-graded algebra
\mathrm{End}_{A}(S)
, as follows.
The proof is straightforward, by using
u\in A^{|\mathrm{type}(A)|}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
S
be an
A
-module and
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathrm{End}_{A}(S)
.
If
A
is nondenerate, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{u}(\{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\})=0.

If
A
is degenerate, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{u}([\xi_{1},\xi_{2}])=0.

Moreover,
\mathrm{Tr}_{u}
is compatible with tensoring negligible modules, as follows.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
V=V^{0}\oplus V^{1}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded real vector space, and
\gamma_{V}
the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading operator on
V
. Let
A
and
S
be as before, and
let
u
be a volume element of
A
.
Then
\gamma_{V}\widehat{\otimes}u
is a volume element of
\mathrm{End}(V)\widehat{\otimes}A
.
For any
\xi\in\mathrm{End}_{A}(S)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{u}(\xi)=\mathrm{Tr}_{\gamma_{V}\widehat{\otimes}u}(\psi^{V}(\xi)).

For later use, we also regard Definition
3.1
as giving a linear map

(3.4)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{A}\colon\mathrm{End}_{A}(S)\to\mathrm{Ori}(A).

3.2.
The case for trivial bundles of
A
-modules

Let
A
be a simple central graded
*
-algebra.
Throughout this subsection, we assume that
A
is nondegenerate.
Let
S
be an
A
-module with an inner product.
Let
X
be a manifold, and consider the trivial
A
-module bundle
\underline{S}
over
X
.
Here, we explain the generalized Clifford superconnection formalism in Subsection
3.3
applied to these settings.

3.2.1.
Generalized Clifford superconnections associated to gradations

Recall that we have introduced the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded algebra structure on
\mathrm{End}_{A}(S)
in Definition
2.8
.
We introduce a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded algebra structure on
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{A}(S))
by the graded tensor product,

\displaystyle\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{A}(S))

\displaystyle=\Omega^{*}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{C^{\infty}(X)}C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{End}_{A}(S)).

This means that the multiplication is given by the formula (
3.33
):

\displaystyle(\omega\widehat{\otimes}\xi)\cdot(\omega^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}\xi^{\prime}):=(-1)^{|\xi|\cdot|\omega^{\prime}|}(\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime})\otimes(\xi\cdot\xi^{\prime}).

The even and odd part of
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{A}(S))
with respect to this grading are denoted by
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{A}(S))^{0}
and
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{A}(S))^{1}
, respectively.
We let the algebra
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{A}(S))
act on
\Omega^{*}(X;S):=C^{\infty}(X;\wedge T^{*}X\otimes S)
by the formula (
3.34
), respecting the grading:

\displaystyle(\omega\widehat{\otimes}\xi)\cdot(\eta\otimes\psi):=(-1)^{|\xi|\cdot|\eta|}(\omega\wedge\eta)\otimes(\xi\cdot\psi).

Given
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}_{A}(S))
, consider the following differential operator on
\Omega^{*}(X;S)
,

(3.5)

\displaystyle\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla:=d+h\colon\Omega^{*}(X;S)\to\Omega^{*}(X;S).

This is an example of a
self-adjoint superconnection
(Definition
3.36
and
3.60
) for the case
(\mathcal{A},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})=(\underline{A},d)
and
\not{S}=\underline{S}
.
The square of the operator (
3.5
) is given by the action of the following element in
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{A}(S))^{0}
,

(3.6)

\displaystyle F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla})=\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{2}=dh+h^{2}\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{A}(S))^{0}.

This is the
twisting curvature
for
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla
in Lemma
3.46
.

As in the case of usual connections, we apply polynomials to
F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla})
and get characteristic forms.
Recall that we defined a linear map
\mathrm{Tr}_{A}\colon\mathrm{End}_{A}(S)\to\mathrm{Ori}(A)
in (
3.4
).
We extend it to a linear map
\mathrm{Tr}_{A}\colon\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{A}(S))\to\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(A))
by applying it to the coefficients (Definition
3.49
).

Definition 3.7

(Definition
3.64
applied to
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla=d+h
)
.

In the above settings,
Pontryagin character form
of
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla=d+h
is defined by

(3.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(d+h):=\mathrm{Tr}_{A}(e^{-F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla})})=\mathrm{Tr}_{A}(e^{-dh-h^{2}})\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)+1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(A)).

The closedness follows from Proposition
3.56
(1), and the fact that the form appears in degrees
\mathrm{type}(A)+1\pmod{4}
follows from Proposition
3.63
below.

The above Pontryagin character form
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(d+h)
depends on
h
, but it is always exact.
Moreover, if we are given an element
h_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Self}_{A}(S))
, which is regarded as a homotopy
h_{I}=\{h_{t}\}_{t\in I}
from
h_{0}
to
h_{1}
, we get an explicit form cobounding
\mathrm{Ph}(d+h_{1})-\mathrm{Ph}(d+h_{0})
, as follows.

Definition 3.9

(Definition
3.64
applied to
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}=d+h
)
.

In the above settings, for an element
h_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Skew}_{A}(S))
, we define its
Chern-Simons form

\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(d_{I\times X}+h_{I})\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(A))
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(d_{I\times X}+h_{I})

\displaystyle:=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(d_{I\times X}+h_{I}\right)

(3.10)

\displaystyle=-\int_{I}dt\wedge\mathrm{Tr}_{A}\left(\frac{dh_{I}}{dt}e^{-d_{X}h_{I}-(h_{I})^{2}}\right).

Here we denote by
d_{I\times X}
and
d_{X}
the de Rham differential on
I\times X
and
X
, respectively.
The last equality in (
3.9
) follows from

\displaystyle(d_{I\times X}+h_{I})^{2}=(d_{X}+h_{I})^{2}+dt\wedge\frac{dh_{I}}{dt}

(also see (
3.58
)).

We have (
3.69
)

(3.11)

\displaystyle d\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(d_{I\times X}+h_{I})=\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(d+h_{1})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(d+h_{0}).

In particular, for any
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}_{A}(S))
, we have the homotopy
h_{I}=th
from
0
to
h
.
Thus, applying (
3.11
) to the homotopy, we see that
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(d+h)
is always exact, as stated above.

3.2.2.
The Pontryagin character forms for gradations

As we have seen in the end of the last subsubsection, the Pontryagin character form
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(d+h)
itself has no interesting cohomological information.
However, the point is that we know a particular choice
\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(d_{I\times X}+th)
of the form cobounding it.
Based on this observation, we introduce the
Pontryagin character form

\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
for
invertible
sections (gradations)
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
, which captures the nontrivial cohomological information.

Assume we are given an invertible section
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
.
Consider the manifold
(0,\infty)\times X
and the section
th\in C^{\infty}((0,\infty)\times X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
, where
t
is the coordinate in
(0,\infty)
.
Applying Definition
3.7
to this, we get

(3.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(d_{(0,\infty)\times X}+th\right)

\displaystyle=-dt\wedge\mathrm{Tr}_{A}\left(he^{-td_{X}h-t^{2}h^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)+1}((0,\infty)\times X;\mathrm{Ori}(A)).

The section
th\in C^{\infty}((0,\infty)\times X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
can be considered as a family of sections
\{th\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))\}_{t\in(0,\infty)}
parametrized by
(0,\infty)
.
The integration of the form (
3.12
) in the
(0,\infty)
-direction can be regarded as a limit of the Chern-Simons form in Definition
3.9
.
As a consequence of the invertibility of the section
h
, we can show that the integration indeed converges.

Lemma 3.13

(The special case of Lemma
3.77
)
.

In the above settings, the following integration converges pointwise, and the resulting form is closed.

(3.14)

\displaystyle-\int_{(0,\infty)}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(d_{(0,\infty)\times X}+th\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{(0,\infty)}dt\wedge\mathrm{Tr}_{A}\left(he^{-td_{X}h-t^{2}h^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(A)).

It turns out that the form (
3.14
) captures the nontrivial cohomological information on
h
.
Recall the homomorphism
\mathcal{R}
given in (
2.57
).

Definition 3.15

(
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
: a special case of Definition
3.80
(1))
.

For
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
, its
Pontryagin character form

\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(A))

is defined by

(3.16)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)

\displaystyle:=-\pi^{-1/2}\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(d_{(0,\infty)\times X}+th)

\displaystyle=\pi^{-1/2}\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}dt\wedge\mathrm{Tr}_{A}\left(he^{-td_{X}h-t^{2}h^{2}}\right).

The cohomology class of
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
only depends on the homotopy class of
h
in
C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
.
Indeed, given an element
h_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
regarded as a homotopy between
h_{0}:=h_{I}|_{\{0\}\times X}
and
h_{1}:=h_{I}|_{\{1\}\times X}
, we get an explicit form cobounding
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0})
, as follows.

Definition 3.17

(
\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I})
: a special case for Definition
3.80
(2))
.

For
h_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
, we define its
Chern-Simons form
by

(3.18)

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I}):=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I}\right)\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(A)).

The fact that
\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I})
satisfies

(3.19)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0})=d\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I})

follows from the closedness of
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
.

3.2.3.
Properties of the Pontryagin character forms

Now we explain the properties of the Pontryagin character form
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
for invertible sections
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
.

To begin with, it is important to notice that
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
is the pullback of the
universal Pontryagin form
on the space
\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S)
.
Indeed, fixing
S
, we have the
universal gradation
, or the
tautological gradation

h_{\mathrm{univ}}
on
\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S)
, given by the identity map,

(3.20)

\displaystyle h_{\mathrm{univ}}:=\mathrm{id}\in C^{\infty}(\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S),\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S)).

We get its Pontryagin character form,

(3.21)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}_{\mathrm{clo}}(\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S);\mathrm{Ori}(A)).

We easily see that Definition
3.15
is contravariant in
X
, and in particular for any
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S))
we have

(3.22)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)=h^{*}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}}).

Thus we call the form
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})
the
universal Pontryagin form
.
The various statements on
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
below are also functorial in
X
, so they can be treated as statements on each
h
, as well as statements on the universal one.
Both viewpoints are useful.

First,
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
is invariant under tensoring negligible modules.
Recall Lemma
2.12
.
Given a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded vector space
V=V^{0}\oplus V^{1}
with a positive definite inner product on each
V^{i}
associating the negligible module
\mathrm{End}(V)
,
for an
A
-module
S
we get the corresponding
\mathrm{End}(V)\widehat{\otimes}A
-module
V\otimes S
, and the isomorphism
\psi^{V}\colon\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S)\simeq\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\mathrm{End}(V)\widehat{\otimes}A}(V\otimes S)
.
The following result follows from a straightforward algebraic computation by using Lemma
3.3
.

Lemma 3.23

(Invariance of
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
under tensoring negligible modules)
.

In the above settings, for
h\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S))
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)=\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\psi^{V}(h)),

where we use the isomorphism
\mathrm{Ori}(A)\simeq\mathrm{Ori}(\mathrm{End}(V)\widehat{\otimes}A)
by
u\mapsto\gamma_{V}\widehat{\otimes}u
.

Next we look at the compatibility with the suspension isomorphism in
KO
-theory.
Recall that, by Fact
2.31
, the suspension isomorphism of
KO
-theory,

(3.24)

\displaystyle KO^{\Sigma^{0,1}A}_{+}(X,Y)\simeq KO^{A}_{+}(X\times I,X\times\partial I\cup Y\times I),

is realized by the map
(S,h_{0},h_{1})\mapsto(S,\widetilde{h}_{0},\widetilde{h}_{1})
.
There, we defined the map

(3.25)

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}^{*}(S))

\displaystyle\to C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S)),

(3.26)

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\mapsto\widetilde{h}(\theta,x)=\beta\cos\pi\theta+h(x)\sin\pi\theta.

As remarked after Fact
2.40
, it is essentially the structure map of the
KO
-spectrum explained in Subsubsection
2.3.2
.
We have the following.

Proposition 3.27
.

For any element
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}^{\dagger}(S))
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\widetilde{h}).

Here we use the isomorphism
\mathrm{Ori}(A)\simeq\mathrm{Ori}(\Sigma^{0,1}A)
given by
u\mapsto u\widehat{\otimes}\beta
.

The proof is done by a direct computation, which we give in Subsection
7.1
.

Finally, we identify the cohomology class of
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
, justifying the name “Pontryagin character form”.
For this, it is convenient to work with the universal one,
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}(\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S);\mathrm{Ori}(A))
, as explained at the beginning of this subsubsection.

Recall that we have the model of the
KO
-spectrum realized as a direct limit of
\mathrm{Skew}_{A}^{*}
given in Subsubsection
2.3.2
.
Letting
\{S_{n}\}_{n}
be any cofinal system of
\Sigma^{0,1}A
-modules,
we have the weak homotopy equivalence (Fact
2.40
),

\displaystyle(KO_{\mathrm{type}(A)},\{*\})\sim\varinjlim_{n}(\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S_{n}),\{\beta\}).

Also recall that this equivalence depends on the choice of a volume element
u\in A
.
Any
\Sigma^{0,1}A
-module
S
admits an inclusion
S\hookrightarrow S_{n}
of
\Sigma^{0,1}A
-modules for
n
large enough, and its image is a direct summand. Any such inclusions are homotopic in the direct limit.
We denote by

(3.28)

\displaystyle\iota_{S,u}\in\left[(\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S),\{\beta\}),(KO_{\mathrm{type}(A)},\{*\})\right]

the homotopy class of the induced map.
The topological Pontryagin character homomorphism (
2.54
) can be regarded as an element

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}\in{H}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}\left(KO_{\mathrm{type}(A)},\{*\};\mathbb{R}\right).

The universal Pontryagin character form (
3.21
) indeed represents the topological Pontryagin character, as follows.

Theorem 3.29
.

Let
A
be a nondegenerate simple central graded
*
-algebra and
S
be a
\Sigma^{0,1}A
-module.
Let
u
be a volume element of
A
, and use it to trivialize
\mathrm{Ori}(A)
.
Then we have the following equality in
H^{4\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(A)}\left(\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S),\{\beta\};\mathbb{R}\right)
,

(3.30)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rham}\left(\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})\right)=\iota_{S,u}^{*}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}.

The proof of Theorem
3.29
is given in Subsection
7.2
.
Using this universal result, we see that
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
realizes the topological Pontryagin character homomorphism for
KO_{+}
, as follows.

Corollary 3.31
.

Let
A
be a nondegenerate simple central graded
*
-algebra and
(X,Y)
be an object of
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
.
Representing classes of
KO_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
by triples
(S,h_{0},h_{1})
with
h_{i}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S))
, we have the following realization of the topological Pontryagin character homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}\colon KO_{+}^{A}(X,Y)

\displaystyle\to H^{4\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(A)}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A)),

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1}]

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Rham}\left(\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0})\right).

Proof.

This follows directly from Theorem
3.29
and the definition (
2.43
) of the isomorphism (
2.42
).
∎

3.3.
The generalized Clifford superconnection formalism

In this subsection, we develop the
generalized Clifford superconnection formalism
.
The construction below can be regarded as a generalization of the (real variant of the) usual
superconnection formalism
, which was developed by Quillen
[
Qui85
]
and has been broadly used since then.
We explain the relation with the Quillen’s formalism in Subsubsection
3.3.1
.
Here we just remark that the Quillen’s
(even) superconnection formalism
corresponds to the case
\mathcal{A}=\underline{Cl_{0,1}}
and the Quillen’s
odd superconnection formalism
corresponds to the case
\mathcal{A}=\underline{Cl_{1,1}}
.

Let
X
be a manifold and
\mathcal{A}
be a bundle of simple central graded
*
-algebras over
\mathbb{R}
on
X
.
Throughout this subsection, we always assume that the fibers of
\mathcal{A}
are
nondegenerate
algebras
5
5

5

When we are interested in bundles of degenerate algebras and modules over them, we tensor negligible modules to produce bundles of nondegenerate algebras, it is enough to use the isomorphism in Lemma
2.12
(3), and then apply the superconnection formalism to the latter.
Actually, the Quillen’s
odd superconnection formalism
can be regarded as such an example.
See Subsubsection
3.3.1
for details.

.

Given an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\not{S}
over a manifold
X
,
we introduce an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle structure on
\wedge T^{*}X\otimes\not{S}
as follows.
For each point
x\in X
, let
\mathcal{A}_{x}
act on
\wedge T^{*}_{x}X\otimes\not{S}_{x}
by the formula

(3.32)

\displaystyle c\cdot(\omega\otimes\psi):=(-1)^{|c|\cdot|\omega|}\omega\otimes(c\cdot\psi).

This makes
\wedge T^{*}X\otimes\not{S}
an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle over
X
.
Let us introduce
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded algebra structures on
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})
and
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
by the graded tensor products,

\displaystyle\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle=\Omega^{*}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{C^{\infty}(X)}C^{\infty}(X;\mathcal{A}).

\displaystyle\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))

\displaystyle=\Omega^{*}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{C^{\infty}(X)}C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})).

This means that the multiplication in these algebras are given by

(3.33)

\displaystyle(\omega\widehat{\otimes}\xi)\cdot(\omega^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}\xi^{\prime}):=(-1)^{|\xi|\cdot|\omega^{\prime}|}(\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime})\otimes(\xi\cdot\xi^{\prime}).

The even and odd part of
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
with respect to this grading are denoted by
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))^{0}
and
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))^{1}
, respectively.
We also use the corresponding notation
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})^{i}
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

Let the algebras
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})
and
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
act on the space of differential forms
\Omega^{*}(X;\not{S})=C^{\infty}(X;\wedge T^{*}X\otimes\not{S})
by

(3.34)

\displaystyle(\omega\widehat{\otimes}\xi)\cdot(\eta\otimes\psi):=(-1)^{|\xi|\cdot|\eta|}(\omega\wedge\eta)\otimes(\xi\cdot\psi).

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\wedge T^{*}X\otimes\not{S})=\wedge T^{*}X\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}).

as bundles of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded algebras.

In the computations below we abuse the notation to use the graded commutators between elements of
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})
and
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
, namely, we denote like

(3.35)

\displaystyle\{\Xi,C\}:=\Xi C-(-1)^{|\Xi||C|}C\Xi

for homogeneous
\Xi\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
and
C\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})
, as an operator on
\Omega^{*}(X;\not{S})
.
Of course, the operator (
3.35
) is zero by definition.

In order to formulate the notion of superconnections on
\not{S}
, we need to fix a connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{A}
which preserves the graded
*
-algebra structures.
Recall that
\mathcal{A}
is associated to a principal
\mathrm{Aut}_{*,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(A)
-bundle
P
over
X
, where
A
denotes the typical fiber of
\mathcal{A}
.
By a
connection on
\mathcal{A}
we mean a connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{A}
induced by an
\mathrm{Aut}_{*,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(A)
-connection on
P
.
By Lemma
2.4
, we know that the set of connections on
\mathcal{A}
is a torsor over
\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
, where
\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}}
is the subbundle of
\mathcal{A}^{0}
consisting of skew-adjoint elements.
Explicitly, given
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and
C\in\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
, the operator

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+\{C,-\}

is another connection on
\mathcal{A}
, and any two connections are related in this way.

Definition 3.36

(
\mathcal{A}
-superconnections)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a bundle of nondegenerate simple central graded
*
-algebras over a manifold
X
, equipped with a connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
Let
\not{S}
be an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle over
X
.
An
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}

is a linear map

\displaystyle\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}\colon\Omega^{*}(X;\not{S})\to\Omega^{*}(X;\not{S})

such that, for any homogeneous element
C\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})
, we have

(3.37)

\displaystyle\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}\circ(C\cdot)-(-1)^{|C|}(C\cdot)\circ\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}=(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}C)\cdot,

where
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})
acts on
\Omega^{*}(X;\not{S})
by (
3.34
).
In particular, if
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
is a usual connection, i.e., increases the form-degree by one, we call it an
\mathcal{A}
-connection
.

In the following, given an
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
, for an element
\Xi\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
or
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})
, we define the operator
\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\Xi\}
on
\Omega^{*}(X;\not{S})
by

(3.38)

\displaystyle\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\Xi\}:=\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}\circ\Xi-(-1)^{|\Xi|}\Xi\circ\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}

if
\Xi
is homogeneous, and extend this definition linearly for general
\Xi
.
Although we are not regarding
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
as an element in any
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded algebra, this convention is useful.

As usual, we can easily show the following.

Lemma 3.39
.

In the settings of Definition
3.36
, for a fixed
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
, the set of
\mathcal{A}
-superconnections on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
is an affine space over
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))^{1}
.

An
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection can be decomposed into a finite sum as

(3.40)

\displaystyle\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}=\nabla+\sum_{j}\omega_{j}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j},

where
\nabla
is an
\mathcal{A}
-connection, each differential form
\omega_{j}
is homogeneous and
\omega_{j}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j}\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))^{1}
.
We are mainly interested in superconnections of the form

\displaystyle\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}=\nabla+\xi_{0},

for
\xi_{0}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{End}^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
.

Lemma 3.41
.

Let us choose and fix
(\mathcal{A},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
in Definition
3.36
. For any
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\not{S}
, there exists an
\mathcal{A}
-connection
\nabla
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proof.

The lemma is shown by taking local trivializations. The key is the fact that any
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle is locally of the form
\underline{S_{0}\otimes W_{0}}
or
\underline{S_{0}\otimes W_{0}}\oplus\underline{S_{1}\otimes W_{1}}
depending on the type of fibers
A
, where
\{S_{i}\}_{i}
is the list of the isomorphism classes of irreducible
A
-modules, and
W_{i}
are some trivial
A
-modules.
∎

It is useful to have the following local description.
Given
X
and
\mathcal{A}
, we can locally (say, on
U
) choose a trivialization
\mathcal{A}\simeq\underline{A}
.
Then any
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
can be written as

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}=d+\{C,-\},

for some
C\in\Omega^{1}(U;A^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
.
Given any
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\not{S}
, by replacing
U
smaller if necessary, we can trivialize it as
\not{S}=\underline{S}
for some
A
-module
S
.
Then the operator

(3.42)

\displaystyle d+C

on
\Omega^{*}(U;S)\simeq\Omega^{*}(U;\not{S})
defines an
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
on
U
.
By Lemma
3.39
, any other
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
on
\not{S}
can be written as

(3.43)

\displaystyle\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}=d+C+B

for some
B\in\Omega^{*}(U;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))^{1}
on
U
.

Now we look at the effects of changes of the connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{A}
.

Lemma 3.44
.

Let
X
and
\mathcal{A}
be as above.
Let
\not{S}
be an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle, and assume we are given an
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
Then, for any
C\in\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
,
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}+C
is an
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+\{C,-\}
.
In other words,
\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
acts on the set of pairs
(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})
consisting of a connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{A}
and an
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
Here
C\in\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
acts as

(3.45)

\displaystyle(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})\mapsto(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+\{C,-\},\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}+C).

In particular, for any two connections
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
and
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
on
\mathcal{A}
, the correspondence (
3.45
) induces a bijection between the space of
\mathcal{A}
-superconnections on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
and that with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
.

Proof.

It is easily shown by the local description above.
∎

Lemma 3.46
.

Let
X
,
\mathcal{A}
,
\not{S}
,
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
be as above.
We have

\displaystyle F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}):=(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})^{2}-(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})^{2}\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))^{0}.

We call
F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
the twisting curvature of
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
.

Proof.

It is enough to show that, for any
C\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})
, we have

\displaystyle F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})\circ(C\cdot)-(C\cdot)\circ F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})=0

as an operator on
\Omega^{*}(X;\not{S})
.
But this is checked directly using (
3.37
).
∎

Remark 3.47
.

In the case of trivial bundles in Subsection
3.2
, we denoted
F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla):=F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla;d)
, where
d
means the trivial connection on
\underline{A}
.
But we omit the reference to
d
when it is obvious.
Similar remarks about the notations also apply to the other objects introduced in Subsection
3.2
.

In the local description (
3.43
),
F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
is given by

(3.48)

\displaystyle F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle=(d+C+B)^{2}-(d+\{C,-\})^{2}

\displaystyle=\{d+C,B\}+B^{2}=dB+B^{2}.

Note that, in particular when
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
is an
\mathcal{A}
-
connection
,
F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
is not the curvature of the connection because we are subtracting
(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})^{2}
.
In fact, it generalizes the
twisting curvature
in the sense of
[
BGV04
, Proposition 3.43]
.

Definition 3.49

(
\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}
)
.

We define a bundle map over
X
,

(3.50)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})\to\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}),

by applying the linear map (
3.4
) fiberwise.
Also extend this map left
\Omega^{*}(X)
-linearly and define

(3.51)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))

\displaystyle\to\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

\displaystyle\omega\widehat{\otimes}\xi

\displaystyle\mapsto\omega\otimes\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\xi).

Note that the map (
3.51
) preserves the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading if
\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})
is odd, and reverses the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading if
\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})
is even.

By Lemma
3.2
, the
u
-trace
\mathrm{Tr}_{u}
is a supertrace on the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded algebra
\mathrm{End}_{A}(S)
.
Using the formula (
3.51
), we easily deduce that

(3.52)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\{\Xi_{1},\Xi_{2}\})=0

for any
\Xi_{1},\Xi_{2}\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
.
We have the following lemma corresponding to
[
Qui85
, Proposition 2]
and
[
BGV04
, Lemma 1.42]
.

Lemma 3.53
.

Let
X
,
\mathcal{A}
,
\not{S}
,
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
be as above.

(1)

For any element
\Xi\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
, we have

\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\Xi\}\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})).

Moreover the homomorphism

(3.54)

\displaystyle\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\cdot\}\colon\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))\to\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))

is an odd graded derivation with respect to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
grading given on
\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
.

(2)

For any element
\Xi\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
, we have

\displaystyle d\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Xi)=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\Xi\}).

Proof.

First we prove (1).
To show the first statement and the fact that (
3.54
) is an odd homomorphism, it is enough to show that

(3.55)

\displaystyle\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\Xi\}C-(-1)^{|C|(|\Xi|+1)}C\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\Xi\}=0

for any homogeneous
\Xi\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
and
C\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})
.
Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\Xi\}C-(-1)^{|C|(|\Xi|+1)}C\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\Xi\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|C||\Xi|}\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},C\}\Xi-(-1)^{|\Xi|}\Xi\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},C\}+\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}\{\Xi,C\}-(-1)^{|\Xi|+|C|}\{\Xi,C\}\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|C||\Xi|}(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}C)\Xi-(-1)^{|\Xi|}\Xi(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}C)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|C||\Xi|}\{\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}C,A\}

\displaystyle=0.

The graded derivation property is obvious.

Next we prove (2).
We use the local description of
\mathcal{A}
-superconnections (
3.43
).
Using the notations there, we have

\displaystyle\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\Xi\}=\{d+C+B,\Xi\}=\{d,\Xi\}+\{B,\Xi\},

since
C\in\Omega^{*}(U;\mathcal{A})
.
By (
3.52
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},\Xi\})=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\{d,\Xi\})=d\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Xi),

so we get the desired result.
∎

Using Lemma
3.53
, by the similar argument as in
[
Qui85
]
and
[
BGV04
]
, we get the following transgression formula, corresponding to
[
BGV04
, Proposition 1.41]
.

Proposition 3.56
.

Let
X
,
\mathcal{A}
and
\not{S}
be as above, and
f(z)\in\mathbb{R}[[z]]
.

(1)

For any connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})))\in\Omega^{*}_{\mathrm{clo}}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

(2)

Consider the manifold
I\times X
and the bundles
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}
and
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\not{S}
on it.
Let
\nabla^{\mathcal{A},I}
be a connection on
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}
and
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}
be an
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}
-superconnection on
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A},I}
.
For
i=0,1
, we set
\nabla^{\mathcal{A},I}|_{\{i\}\times X}=\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
and
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}|_{\{i\}\times X}=\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{i}
. Then we have

d\int_{I}\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}}\left(f\left(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A},I})\right)\right)\\
=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{1}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{1})))-\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{0}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{0}))).

(3)

Let
\{\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\}_{t}
and
\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{t}\}_{t}
be a smooth
1
-parameter family of connections on
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{A}
-superconnections on
\not{S}
compatible with it.
We have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{t}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{t})))=d\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\left(\frac{d\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{t}}{dt}-\frac{d\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{t}}{dt}\right)f^{\prime}(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{t}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{t}))\right).

Proof.

For (1), using the local description (
3.43
) we have

\displaystyle\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})\}

\displaystyle=-\{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}},(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})^{2}\}=-\{d+C+B,(d+\{C,-\})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=-\{B,(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})^{2}\}=0,

where the last equality is because
B\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
and
(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})^{2}\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathcal{A})
.
The formula above and Lemma
3.53
lead to (1).

For (2), we can decompose
\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}}\left(f\left(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A},I})\right)\right)
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}}\left(f\left(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A},I})\right)\right)=dt\wedge\alpha(t)+\beta(t),

where
t
is the coordinate on
I=[0,1]
and
\alpha(t),\beta(t)\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))
.
We have
\beta(i)=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F_{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla_{i}}))
for
i=0,1
.
By (1) we know that

\displaystyle 0=-dt\wedge d_{X}\alpha+dt\wedge\frac{d\beta}{dt}+d_{X}\beta,

so that

(3.57)

\displaystyle d_{X}\alpha=\frac{d\beta}{dt}.

We have

\displaystyle d\int_{I}\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}}\left(f\left(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A},I})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=d\int_{I}dt\wedge\alpha

\displaystyle=\int_{I}dt\wedge d_{X}\alpha

\displaystyle=\int_{I}dt\wedge\frac{d\beta}{dt}

\displaystyle=\beta(1)-\beta(0)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{1}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{1})))-\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{0}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{0}))),

so we get (2).

For (3), as a special case of (2) we consider the connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{A},I}=d_{t}+\nabla_{t}^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}=d_{t}+\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{t}
, where
d_{t}
is the de Rham differential on
[0,1]
.
We have

\displaystyle(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I})^{2}

\displaystyle=(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{t})^{2}+dt\wedge\frac{d\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{t}}{dt},

\displaystyle(\nabla^{\mathcal{A},I})^{2}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{t}^{V})^{2}+dt\wedge\frac{d\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{t}}{dt},

which imply

(3.58)

\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(f\left(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A},I})\right)\right)=\\
dt\wedge\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\left(\frac{d\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{t}}{dt}-\frac{d\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{t}}{dt}\right)f^{\prime}(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{t}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{t}))\right)+\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}_{t}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}_{t}))).

The computation is parallel to
[
BGV04
, pp.48–49]
.
By (
3.57
) and (
3.58
), we get (3).
∎

The form
\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(f(F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}))\right)
is invariant under the action of
\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
in Lemma
3.44
, as follows.

Lemma 3.59
.

In the above settings, let
C\in\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
.
For any
f(z)\in\mathbb{R}[[z]]
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(f(F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}))\right)=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(f(F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}+C;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+\{C,-\}))\right).

Proof.

It follows from the application of Proposition
3.56
(3) to the linear path from
(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})
to
(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+\{C,-\},\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}+C)
.
∎

Next we introduce the self and skew-adjointness condition on
\mathcal{A}
-superconnections.

Definition 3.60

(Self-adjointness and Skew-adjointness of generalized Clifford superconnections)
.

In the above settings, assume that
\not{S}
is equipped with an inner product.
Let
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
be an
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection.

(1)

We say that
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
is
self-adjoint
if, in a decomposition (
3.40
),
\nabla^{\not{S}}
preserves the inner product,
\xi_{j}
is skew-adjoint if
|\omega_{j}|\equiv 1,2\pmod{4}
and self-adjoint if
|\omega_{j}|\equiv 0,3\pmod{4}
.

(2)

We say that
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
is
skew-adjoint
if, in a decomposition (
3.40
),
\nabla^{\not{S}}
preserves the inner product,
\xi_{j}
is skew-adjoint if
|\omega_{j}|\equiv 0,1\pmod{4}
and self-adjoint if
|\omega_{j}|\equiv 2,3\pmod{4}
.

This definition does not depend on the choice of the decomposition.

Remark 3.61
.

Definition
3.60
is motivated by the self/skew-adjointness of the associated Dirac operators, as follows.
Let
X
be a manifold with a Riemannian metric
g
.
First we consider the Clifford algebra bundle
C(T^{*}X,g)
, in which the Clifford multiplications of cotangent vectors are skew-adjoint (see (
2.1
)).
Let
\not{S}
be a
C(T^{*}X,g)
-module bundle with an inner product.
Assume we have a
self-adjoint

C(T^{*}X,g)
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
on
\not{S}
compatible with the Levi-Civita connection.
Then the associated
Dirac operator
is given by the following composition
6
6

6

The complex analogue of (
3.62
) generalizes the twisted Dirac operators defined in
[
Kah11
]
.
In that paper, the author considers the case where we have a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded hermitian vector bundle
E
equipped with a Quillen’s superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{E}
,
and Spin
c
-Dirac operators twisted by
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{E}
.
In our language, such
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{E}
induces a
C(T^{*}X,g)
-superconnection on
\not{S}\otimes E
, where
\not{S}
is the Spinor bundle on a Spin
c
-manifold, and (
3.62
) recovers the one in
[
Kah11
]
.

.

(3.62)

\displaystyle\not{D}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})\colon C^{\infty}(X;\not{S})\xrightarrow{\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}}\Omega^{*}(X;\not{S})\xrightarrow{c}C^{\infty}(X;\not{S}).

Here the second map is the Clifford multiplication.
Then it is easily checked that the self-adjointness of
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
implies that
\not{D}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})
is formally self-adjoint.

Next we consider the Clifford algebra bundle
(T^{*}X,-g)
, where now the Clifford multiplications of cotangent vectors are self-adjoint.
Let
\not{S}
be a
C(T^{*}X,-g)
-module bundle with an inner product, and assume we have a
skew-adjoint

C(T^{*}X,-g)
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
on
\not{S}
compatible with the Levi-Civita connection.
Then the associated Dirac operator
\not{D}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})
is defined by the same formula as (
3.62
).
In this case, the skew-adjointness of
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
implies that
\not{D}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})
is formally skew-adjoint.
We also note that the
massive Dirac operator
in (
1.8
) can be written in terms of this construction.

Proposition 3.63
.

Assume that an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\not{S}
is equipped with an inner product and an
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
Let
f(z)\in\mathbb{R}[[z]]
.

(1)

If
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
is self-adjoint, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})))\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})+1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

(2)

If
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
is skew-adjoint, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})))\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

The proof is by a direct computation, which we give in Subsection
7.3
.

Definition 3.64

(
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
,
\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
)
.

Let
X
,
\mathcal{A}
,
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\not{S}
be as above.

(1)

For a self-adjoint
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
, we define its
Pontryagin character form
by

(3.65)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}):=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(e^{-F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})})\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})+1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

For a self-adjoint
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}
on the
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\not{S}
over
I\times X
compatible with
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
, we define its
Chern-Simons form
by

(3.66)

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}):=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I};\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}\right)\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

(2)

In the same settings, if
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
is skew-adjoint, we define its
Pontryagin character form
by

(3.67)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}):=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(e^{F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})})\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

If
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}
is skew-adjoint, we define its
Chern-Simons form
by

(3.68)

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}):=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}\left(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I};\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}\right)\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-2}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

By Proposition
3.56
, setting
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla_{i}^{\not{S}}:=\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}|_{\{i\}\times X}
we have

(3.69)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla_{1}^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla_{0}^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})=d\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}).

By Lemma
3.59
, we get the following.

Lemma 3.70
.

For any
C\in\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})=\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}+C;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+\{C,-\}).

Moreover, if we have an
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}
as in Definition
3.64
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})=\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}+\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}C;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+\{C,-\}).

3.3.1.
The relation with the superconnection formalism by Quillen
[
Qui85
]

Here we explain how to recover the Quillen’s superconnection formalism
[
Qui85
]
as a special case of the formalisms developed in this subsection.

First of all, Quillen works in the
\mathbb{C}
-linear setting.
Thus, precisely speaking we recover the
\mathbb{R}
-linear version of Quillen’s superconnection formalism (by the obvious restriction of the coefficients
\mathbb{R}\subset\mathbb{C}
) as a special case of the formalisms in this subsection.
As we will explain in Subsection
6.1
, the formalism in this subsection can be modified to the
\mathbb{C}
-linear setting in a straightforward way.
Based on the modified formalism, we can generalize the following discussion in a straightforward manner to recover the formalism in
[
Qui85
]
.

Recall that in
[
Qui85
]
the superconnections are considered for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded vector bundles (which we call the
even superconnection formalism
) and ungraded vector bundles (which we call the
odd superconnection formalism
).

For the former, the relation with our formulation is very simple.
The even superconnection formalism is our superconnection formalism in the case where
\mathcal{A}
is the trivial bundle with fiber
Cl_{0,1}
.
Indeed, a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading is equivalent to a
Cl_{0,1}
-module structure, and the trace
\mathrm{Tr}_{u}
for a volume element
u\in Cl_{0,1}
recovers the usual supertrace.

Now we explain the latter.
The odd superconnection formalism can be regarded as our superconnection formalism in the case where
\mathcal{A}
is the trivial bundle with fiber
Cl_{1,1}
, as follows.
In
[
Qui85
, Section 5]
, we start with an ungraded vector bundle
\not{S}
, form a vector bundle
\not{S}^{\prime}:=Cl_{0,1}\otimes\not{S}
, regard
\not{S}^{\prime}
as a right
Cl_{0,1}
-module, and consider the bundle
\mathrm{End}_{\sigma}(\not{S}^{\prime})
of endomorphisms on
\not{S}^{\prime}
commuting with the right
Cl_{0,1}
-action, where
\sigma\in Cl_{0,1}
denotes a generator.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{End}_{\sigma}(\not{S}^{\prime})=\{\mathrm{id}\otimes a+(\sigma\cdot)\otimes b\ |\ a,b\in\mathrm{End}({\not{S}})\}.

Then the
odd supertrace
is defined as

(3.71)

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\sigma}\colon\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\sigma}(\not{S}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\to\Omega^{*}(X)

\displaystyle\mathrm{id}\otimes a+(\sigma\cdot)\otimes b

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Tr}_{\not{S}}(b).

Now we turn to our formalism.
Recall that in the generalized Clifford superconnection formalism of this subsection, we required that
\mathcal{A}
is a bundle of nondegenerete algebras.
Thus, for an ungraded vector bundle
{\not{S}}
regarded as an
\mathbb{R}=Cl_{0,0}
-module bundle, we cannot apply the formalism directly.
In such a case, we tensor a negligible module to produce a nondegenerate algebra, use the isomorphism in Lemma
2.12
(3), and apply the superconnection formalism to the latter.
Actually, the above process of producing
\mathrm{tr}_{\sigma}
on
\mathrm{End}_{\sigma}(\not{S}^{\prime})
can be regarded as performing this procedure.
Indeed, consider the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded vector space
V=V^{0}\oplus V^{1}=\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{R}
and the associated negligible module
\mathrm{End}(V)\simeq Cl_{1,1}
.
The grading operator
\gamma_{V}
on
V
is a volume element for
\mathrm{End}(V)
.
Then, given an ungraded vector bundle
{\not{S}}
, as in Lemma
2.12
(3) we consider the
\mathrm{End}(V)
-module bundle
V\otimes{\not{S}}
.
The bundle
V\otimes{\not{S}}
corresponds to the above
\not{S}^{\prime}=Cl_{0,1}\otimes\not{S}
, and the action by
\gamma_{V}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\not{S}}
corresponds to
(\sigma\cdot)\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\not{S}}
7
7

7

Be careful that in this correspondence the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading on
V
does not correspond to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading on
Cl_{0,1}
.
In our generalized Clifford superconnection formalism, as an
\mathrm{End}(V)
-module we just regard
V\otimes{\not{S}}
as an ungraded module, so this difference is irrelevant.

.
We have

(3.72)

\displaystyle\mathrm{End}^{0}_{\mathrm{End}(V)}(V\otimes\not{S})

\displaystyle=\{\mathrm{id}_{V}\otimes a\ |\ a\in\mathrm{End}(\not{S})\},

(3.73)

\displaystyle\mathrm{End}^{1}_{\mathrm{End}(V)}(V\otimes\not{S})

\displaystyle=\{\gamma_{V}\otimes b\ |\ b\in\mathrm{End}(\not{S})\},

and the
\mathrm{Tr}_{\gamma_{V}}
in Definition
3.1
is given by

(3.74)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\gamma_{V}}\colon\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}^{1}_{\mathrm{End}(V)}(V\otimes\not{S}))

\displaystyle\to\Omega^{*}(X)

\displaystyle\mathrm{id}_{V}\otimes a+\gamma_{V}\otimes b

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Tr}_{\not{S}}(b).

Thus we see that we recover the odd supertrace
\mathrm{tr}_{\sigma}
in (
3.71
), as desired.

3.4.
The Pontryagin character forms for gradations

Let
X
,
\mathcal{A}
,
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\not{S}
with an inner product be as in the last subsection.
Note that we are assuming that the fibers of
\mathcal{A}
are nondegenerate.
Let
\nabla^{\not{S}}
be a self-adjoint
\mathcal{A}
-
connection
(i.e., one which increases the form degree by one) on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
In this setting, given a smooth invertible section
h\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we are going to define its
Pontryagin character form

\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
(Definition
3.80
), generalizing Definition
3.15
.

Consider the manifold
(0,\infty)_{t}\times X
, and the bundles
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}
and
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\not{S}
on it.
We consider the following
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}
-superconnection on
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\not{S}
compatible with
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
,

(3.75)

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th,

where
t
denotes the corrdinate on
(0,\infty)
.
Then (
3.75
) is self-adjoint in the sense of Definition
3.60
, so we have

(3.76)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})+1}((0,\infty)\times X;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Lemma 3.77
.

In the above settings, the following integration converges pointwise,

(3.78)

\displaystyle\int_{(0,\infty)}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

We have

(3.79)

\displaystyle d\int_{(0,\infty)}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})=-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}).

Proof.

Working locally, we may trivialize
\mathcal{A}
and
\not{S}
.
By Lemma
3.70
and the fact that under the action (
3.45
) the superconnection (
3.75
) transforms as
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th\mapsto\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}(\nabla^{\not{S}}+C)+th
, we may assume
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}=d
.
In this case we have
(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th)^{2}\in\Omega^{*}((0,\infty)\times X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
, and
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th)=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(e^{-(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th)^{2}})
.
Fix any Riemannian metric on
X
.
It is enough to show that, for each
x\in X
, there exists
c>0
and
t_{0}\in(0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\|e^{-(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th)^{2}}\|<e^{-t^{2}c}

on
[t_{0},\infty)\times\{x\}
.
We have

\displaystyle(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th)^{2}=h+(\nabla^{\not{S}})^{2}+t\{\nabla^{\not{S}},h\}+t^{2}h^{2}.

Since
h(x)
is self-adjoint and invertible, there exists a constant
a>0
such that
h(x)^{2}>a\cdot\mathrm{Id}
.
From this we get the convergence of (
3.78
).
(
3.79
) follows from (
3.69
) and the convergence
\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\nabla^{\not{S}}+th;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})=0
shown similarly.
∎

By Lemma
3.77
, we get

\displaystyle-\pi^{-1/2}\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Here
\mathcal{R}
is the endomorphism on differential forms defined in (
2.57
).

Definition 3.80

(
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
,
\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
)
.

Let
X
,
\mathcal{A}
,
\not{S}
,
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\nabla^{\not{S}}
be as above.

(1)

For any smooth invertible section
h\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we define

(3.81)

\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}):=-\pi^{-1/2}\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})\\
\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

The convergence of the integration follows from Lemma
3.77
.
Note that the form above is not a closed one (see (
3.79
)).

(2)

For any smooth invertible section
h_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we define

(3.82)

\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}):=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I};\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\\
\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Lemma 3.83
.

We have

(3.84)

d\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\\
=\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I}|_{\{1\}\times X};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I}|_{\{0\}\times X};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}\right).

Proof.

Based on (
3.79
), the difference of the left and the right hand side of (
3.84
) is computed as

\displaystyle\int_{I}d\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I};\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)

\displaystyle=(\mbox{const})\cdot\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}};\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle=(\mbox{const})\cdot\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{I}\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})=0.

∎

Note that, in Definition
3.80
(1), if we have a submanifold
Y\subset X
and two smooth sections
h_{0},h_{1}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
such that
h_{0}|_{Y}=h_{1}|_{Y}
, we have

(3.85)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}_{\mathrm{clo}}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

The closedness follows from (
3.79
).
Similarly, in Definition
3.80
(2), if
h_{I}|_{I\times Y}
is constant in the
I
-direction, we have

\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})|_{Y}=0
, so that we have

(3.86)

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Moreover, we can also easily show that the above Chern-Simons form modulo exact forms only depends on the homotopy class of the homotopy
h_{I}
, that is, if we have two choices of homotopies
h_{I}
and
h^{\prime}_{I}
which are homotopic in the obvious sense, then we have

(3.87)

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})-\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h^{\prime}_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})\in\mathrm{Im}(d).

Now we investigate into properties of
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
.
Our goal is Theorem
3.96
, which says that
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
realizes the twisted topological Pontryagin character homomorphism in Subsection
2.6
.
It generalizes Corollary
3.31
.

First, by Lemma
3.70
we get the following.

Lemma 3.88
.

For any
C\in\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+C,\nabla^{\not{S}}+C),

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+C,\nabla^{\not{S}}+C).

Next, we show that the cohomology class of (
3.85
) does not depend on the connections, as follows.

Lemma 3.89
.

Let
X
,
\mathcal{A}
and
\not{S}
be as above.
Assume we have a submanifold
Y\subset X
and two smooth sections
h_{0},h_{1}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
such that
h_{0}|_{Y}=h_{1}|_{Y}
.
Then the cohomology class of the difference element (
3.85
),

(3.90)

\mathrm{Rham}\left(\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})\right)\\
\in H^{4\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(\mathcal{A})}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})),

does not depend on the compatible pair
(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
.

Proof.

Any two compatible pairs
(\nabla_{0}^{V},\nabla_{0}^{\not{S}})
and
(\nabla_{1}^{V},\nabla_{1}^{\not{S}})
are connected by a homotopy.
As always, we can transgress the difference of the forms (
3.85
) corresponding to those connections, by integrating the form (
3.85
) constructed by using the homotopy in the
I
-direction.
∎

Recall that twists in
KO_{+}
by negligible bundles are trivial (Fact
2.33
).
Here we show the corresponding statement for the Pontryagin character forms.
Let
E=E^{0}\oplus E^{1}
be a smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded vector bundle over
X
equipped with a fiberwise positive definite inner product on each
E^{i}
, associating the negligible bundle
\mathrm{End}(E)
.
Recall Lemma
2.12
.
Given any other
\mathcal{A}
, for an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\not{S}
with an inner product we get an
\mathrm{End}(E)\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
E\otimes\not{S}
, and we have a canonical isomorphism
\psi^{E}\colon\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})\simeq\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\mathrm{End}(E)\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{A}}(E\otimes\not{S})
.

Lemma 3.91
.

In the above setting,
let
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
be a connection on
\mathcal{A}
and
\nabla^{\not{S}}
be an
\mathcal{A}
-connection on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
Let
\nabla^{E}
be any orthogonal connection on
E
, and we denote by
\nabla^{\mathrm{End}(E)}
the induced connection on
\mathrm{End}(E)
.
Then
\nabla^{E}\otimes 1+1\otimes\nabla^{\not{S}}
is an
\mathrm{End}(E)\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{A}
-connection on
\mathrm{End}(E)\otimes\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathrm{End}(E)}\otimes 1+1\otimes\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
. Furthermore, for any
h\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
, we have

(3.92)

\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})\\
=\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\psi^{E}(h);\nabla^{\mathrm{End}(E)}\otimes 1+1\otimes\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{E}\otimes 1+1\otimes\nabla^{\not{S}}).

Here we are using
\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\mathrm{Ori}(\mathrm{End}(E)\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{A})
given by
u\mapsto\gamma_{E}\widehat{\otimes}u
.

Proof.

Locally we can trivialize
E
and write
\nabla^{E}=d+C^{E}
for some
\mathrm{End}(E)^{0}
-valued one-form
C^{E}
.
Then we have
\nabla^{\mathrm{End}(E)}=d+\{C^{E},-\}
, so the result follows from Lemma
3.88
and Lemma
3.3
.
∎

Now we turn to the relation with the twisted topological Pontryagin character homomorphism in Subsection
2.6
.
For this, we use the explanation of the twisted
KO
-theory in Subsection
2.5
in terms of
\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(-)
-bundles.

Recall that in the untwisted case, Pontryagin character forms can be regarded as the pullbacks of the universal Pontryagin form (
3.21
).
Here in the twisted case, we also have the corresponding universal form.
Let
X
,
\mathcal{A}
and
\not{S}
be as before, and consider the fiber bundle
\pi\colon\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S})\to X
.
We consider the pullback of
\mathcal{A}
and
\not{S}
on
\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S})
.
Then we have the
universal gradation
, which is given by the tautological section,

\displaystyle h^{\mathcal{A},\not{S}}_{\mathrm{univ}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S});\mathrm{Self}_{\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\pi^{*}\not{S})).

Given a pair
(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
as before, we pull them back by
\pi
, and get the
universal Pontryagin form
,

(3.93)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h^{\mathcal{A},\not{S}}_{\mathrm{univ}};\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\pi^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S});\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})),

where we abuse the notation to write the pullback of
\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})
by the same symbol.
Note that it is not closed (
3.79
).
It is universal in the following sense.
Any section
h\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
is in particular a smooth map from
X
to
\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S})
, and the gradation
h
is the pullback of the universal gradation
h^{\mathcal{A},\not{S}}_{\mathrm{univ}}
by this map.
By the obvious naturality of the Pontryagin character forms, we see that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})=h^{*}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h^{\mathcal{A},\not{S}}_{\mathrm{univ}};\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\pi^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}\right).

Recall that, as explained in Subsection
2.5
, twisted
KO
-theory groups are given in terms of sections of bundles of based spaces.
As explained around (
2.66
), it corresponds to considering pairs
(\not{S},h_{0})
of an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle and a section
h_{0}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
.
For such a pair, we get a closed form,

(3.94)

\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h^{\mathcal{A},\not{S}}_{\mathrm{univ}};\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\pi^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(\pi^{*}h_{0};\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\pi^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\\
\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}),\{h_{0}\};\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

The cohomology class of the form (
3.94
) is independent of
(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
by Lemma
3.89
, which we denote by

(3.95)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\mathcal{A},\not{S},h_{0})\in H^{4\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(\mathcal{A})}(\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}),\{h_{0}\};\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Theorem 3.96
.

Let
X
be connected.
The class (
3.95
) is the pullback of the universal topological Pontryagin character class

\displaystyle[\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}]\in H^{4\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(\mathcal{A})}((\mathcal{KO}_{\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})})_{\iota},\{\beta\};E\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathbb{R})

in (
2.74
) under the map given as the composition

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}),\{h_{0}\})\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{KO}_{\mathcal{A}},\{\beta\})\to((\mathcal{KO}_{\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})})_{\iota},\{\beta\}),

where the first inclusion map is (
2.67
) and the second map comes from the composition of the classifying map
X\to B\mathrm{Aut}_{*,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(A)
of
\mathcal{A}
and the map
(w_{1},w_{2})\colon B\mathrm{Aut}_{*,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(A)\to B\mathrm{O}\langle 0,1,2\rangle
.

Proof.

We use the notations in Subsection
2.6
.
By the naturality of the Pontryagin character forms, the class (
3.95
) gives an element

(3.97)

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(V,\not{S},h_{0})\}_{(S,h_{0})}

\displaystyle\in\varprojlim_{(\not{S},h_{0})}H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}\left(\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}),\{h_{0}\};\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(\mathcal{KO}_{\mathcal{A}},\{\beta\};\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Here the inverse limit runs over the pairs
(\not{S},h_{0})
of
\mathcal{A}
-module bundles with inner product and sections
h_{0}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\dagger}(\not{S}))
with respect to their inclusions.
The isomorphism in (
3.97
) is given by Lemma
2.68
.
Moreover, by the same naturality, we see that the class (
3.97
) is natural in
(X,\mathcal{A})
.
Since
B\mathrm{Aut}_{*,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(A)
can be realized as a direct limit of closed manifolds, we get the element

(3.98)

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{Ph}(V,\not{S},h_{0})\}_{(X,V,S,h_{0})}\in H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(\mathcal{KO}_{E\mathrm{Aut}_{*,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(A)},\{\beta\};\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Here we abuse the notation to denote by
\mathcal{KO}_{E\mathrm{Aut}_{*,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(A)}
the bundle over
B\mathrm{Aut}_{*,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(A)
defined by applying
(
2.64
) to
P=E\mathrm{Aut}_{*,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(A)
.
In turn, by Lemma
3.91
this element is the pullback under
(w_{1},w_{2})\colon\mathcal{KO}_{E\mathrm{Aut}_{*,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(A)}\to(\mathcal{KO}_{\mathrm{type}(A)})_{\iota}
of an element on
(\mathcal{KO_{\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}})_{\iota}
denoted temporarily as

(3.99)

\displaystyle[\mathrm{Ph}^{\prime}]\in H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}((\mathcal{KO}_{\mathrm{type}(A))})_{\iota},\{\beta\};E\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathbb{R}).

Moreover, the element (
3.99
) restricts to the universal class for the untwisted topological Pontryagin character

[\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}]\in H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(KO_{\mathrm{type}(A)},\{*\};\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))

on each fiber.
To see this, it is enough to show the corresponding statement for any of
\mathcal{KO}_{\mathcal{A}}
.
When
X=\mathrm{pt}
, the element (
3.95
) coincides with the untwisted universal Pontryagin form in (
3.21
).
Using Theorem
3.29
and taking the inverse limit, we get the desired claim.

By Lemma
2.75
, this property establishes

\displaystyle[\mathrm{Ph}^{\prime}]=[\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}],

which leads to the theorem.
∎

Recall that, for a CW-pair
(X,Y)
, given
\mathcal{A}
over
X
, we have (
2.73
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}\colon KO^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)\to H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Theorem
3.96
and the definition of the isomorphism (
2.66
) imply the following.

Corollary 3.100
.

In the setting of Definition
3.80
,
let
(X,Y)
be an object of
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
.
Suppose we have two sections
h_{0},h_{1}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
such that
h_{0}|_{Y}=h_{1}|_{Y}
.
Then we have

(3.101)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}([\not{S},h_{0},h_{1}])=\mathrm{Rham}\left(\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})\right).

Here we note that the right hand side is well-defined by (
3.85
).

4.
The definition of differential
KO
-theory
\widehat{KO}_{+}

In this section we define our model of differential
KO
groups.
We deal with the untwisted case in Subsection
4.1
, and the twisted case in Subsection
4.2
.
In this section we work over
\mathbb{R}
.

4.1.
The untwisted groups
\widehat{KO}^{A}_{+}

In this subsection we give a model
\widehat{KO}^{A}_{+}
for the untwisted differential
KO
-theory groups, by refining Definition
2.21
.
In this subsection we use the notations in Subsection
3.2
.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
A
be a nondegenerate simple central graded
*
-algebra.
Let
(X,Y)
be an object in
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
.

•

A
\widehat{KO}_{+}
-cocycle

(S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta)
on
(X,Y)
consists of an
A
-module
S
with an inner product, two smooth maps
h_{0},h_{1}\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S))
such that
h_{0}|_{Y}=h_{1}|_{Y}
, and an element

\eta\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d).

•

Two
\widehat{KO}_{+}
-cocycles
(S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta)
and
(S^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{0},h^{\prime}_{1},\eta^{\prime})
are
isomorphic

if there exists an isometric isomorphism of
A
-modules
f\colon S\simeq S^{\prime}
such that
f\circ h_{i}=h^{\prime}_{i}\circ f
for
i=0,1
, and we have
\eta=\eta^{\prime}
.

Definition 4.2

(
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
)
.

Let
A
and
(X,Y)
be as in Definition
4.1

•

We introduce an abelian monoid structure on the set
\widehat{M}^{A}_{+}(X,Y)
of isomorphism classes of
\widehat{KO}_{+}
-cocycles
(S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta)
on
(X,Y)
by

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]+[S^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{0},h^{\prime}_{1},\eta^{\prime}]=[S\oplus S^{\prime},h_{0}\oplus h^{\prime}_{0},h_{1}\oplus h^{\prime}_{1},\eta+\eta^{\prime}].

•

We define
\widehat{Z}^{A}_{+}(X,Y)
to be the submonoid of
\widehat{M}^{A}_{+}(X,Y)
consisting of elements of the form

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I})],

where
h_{I}
is a smooth homotopy between
h_{0}
and
h_{1}
which is constant on
Y
, i.e., a smooth map
h_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
with
h_{I}|_{\{i\}\times X}=h_{i}
for
i=0,1
and
h_{I}|_{\{t\}\times Y}=h_{0}|_{Y}
for all
t\in I
.

•

We define
\widehat{KO}^{A}_{+}(X,Y):=\widehat{M}^{A}_{+}(X,Y)/\widehat{Z}^{A}_{+}(X,Y)
.

Lemma 4.3
.

\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
is an abelian group.

Proof.

The additive inverse of an element
[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\in\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
is given as follows.
Consider the
A
-module bundle
S\oplus S
, and note that
h_{0}\oplus h_{1}
and
h_{1}\oplus h_{0}
are homotopic relative to
Y
in
C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S\oplus S))
.
Take any smooth homotopy
h_{I}
between them, which is constant on
Y
.
Then we have the following equality in
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
.

\displaystyle\left[S\oplus S,h_{0}\oplus h_{1},h_{1}\oplus h_{0},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I}\right)\right]=0.

This means that we have

(4.4)

\displaystyle-[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]=\left[S,h_{1},h_{0},-\eta+\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I}\right)\right].

∎

The abelian group
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
has the obvious naturality in
(X,Y)
, so we regard
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}
as a contravariant functor,

(4.5)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}\colon\mathrm{MfdPair}^{\mathrm{op}}_{f}\to\mathrm{Ab}.

The remainder of this subsection is devoted to showing that
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}
satisfies the axioms of differential extension as developed by Bunke and Schick
[
BS09
,
BS12
]
. First of all, we define the structure homomorphisms for
\widehat{KO}^{A}_{+}
as follows.

Definition 4.6

(Structure homomorphisms for
\widehat{KO}^{A}_{+}
)
.

We define the following structure homomorphisms, which are natural in
(X,Y)
.

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\colon\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)\to\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\mapsto\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0})+d\eta.

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\colon\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)\to KO_{+}^{A}(X,Y)

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\mapsto[S,h_{0},h_{1}].

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\colon\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d)\to\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)

\displaystyle\eta\mapsto[0,0,0,\eta].

The well-definedness of
R
follows from (
3.19
).

Now we check that the functor
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}
satisfies the axioms for the differential
KO^{\mathrm{type}(A)}
.

Theorem 4.7
.

In the notations of Definition
4.6
, we have the following.

(1)

We have
R\circ a=d
.

(2)

The following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{I}
\textstyle{\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(A)}_{\mathrm{clo}}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Rham}}
\textstyle{KO_{+}^{A}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}}
\textstyle{H^{4\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(A)}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A)).}

(3)

The following sequence is exact.

\textstyle{KO_{+}^{\Sigma^{0,1}A}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I}
\textstyle{KO_{+}^{A}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Proof.

(1) is obvious.
(2) follows from Corollary
3.31
.
We prove (3).
The exactness of (
3
) at
{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
, i.e., the surjectivity of
I
, is obvious.
We then check the exactness at
\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d)
.
We have the following commutative diagram.

\textstyle{KO_{+}^{A}(I\times X,\partial I\times X\cup I\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{susp}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}(I\times X,\partial I\times X\cup I\times Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{susp}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{KO_{+}^{\Sigma^{0,1}A}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}}
\textstyle{H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))}

We would like to apply (2) to the top row of the above diagram, but a technical point is that the subset
\partial I\times X\cup I\times Y
is not a manifold.
So we choose smooth neighborhood
U
of
\partial I\times X\cup I\times Y
in
I\times X
which has a deformation retraction to
\partial I\times X\cup I\times Y
.
Then by (2) and the surjectivity of
I
, we have the following commutative diagram.

\textstyle{\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(I\times X,\overline{U})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{I}
\textstyle{\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}(I\times X,\overline{U};\mathrm{Ori}(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Rham}}
\textstyle{KO_{+}^{A}(I\times X,\overline{U})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}(I\times X,\overline{U};\mathrm{Ori}(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{KO_{+}^{A}(I\times X,\partial I\times X\cup I\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}}
\textstyle{H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}(I\times X,\partial I\times X\cup I\times Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))}

Thus, noting that the suspension in the de Rham cohomology is given by the integration
\int_{I}
on closed forms, the desired exactness is equivalent to the exactness of the following sequence.

(4.22)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(I\times X,\overline{U})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Rham}\circ\int_{I}\circ R}\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d)\xrightarrow{a}\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y).

Now we prove that the composition
a\circ\mathrm{Rham}\circ\int_{I}\circ R
in (
4.22
) is zero.
By the commutativity of the upper square of (
4.1
), the image under
\mathrm{Rham}\circ\int_{I}\circ R
of any element in
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(I\times X,\overline{U})
only depends on its image in
KO_{+}^{A}(I\times X,\overline{U})
under
I
.
Moreover, by the excision, we know that the group
KO_{+}^{A}(I\times X,\overline{U})
is generated by triples of the form

(4.23)

\displaystyle(S,h_{0},h_{1}),

where
h_{0},h_{1}
are smooth and
h_{0}|_{I\times Y}=h_{1}|_{I\times Y}
is
constant
in the
I
-direction, i.e., there exists a smooth map
h_{Y}\in C^{\infty}(Y,\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S))
such that
h_{i}|_{\{t\}\times Y}=h_{Y}
for all
t\in[0,1]
,
i=0,1
.
It is enough to prove that the element

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1},0]\in\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(I\times X,\overline{U})

maps to zero under the composition (
4.22
).
We have

\displaystyle\int_{I}\circ R[S,h_{0},h_{1},0]=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1})-\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0}).

So we have

\displaystyle a\circ\mathrm{Rham}\int_{I}\circ R[S,h_{0},h_{1},0]=\left[0,0,0,\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1})-\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0})\right].

To see that this element is zero, notice that, for each
i=0,1
the following
\widehat{KO}^{A}_{+}
-cocycle is an element in
\widehat{Z}^{A}_{+}(X,Y)
,

\displaystyle[S,h_{i}|_{\{0\}\times X},h_{i}|_{\{1\}\times X},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{i})]\in\widehat{Z}^{A}_{+}(X,Y),

where
h_{i}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
is regarded as a homotopy from
h_{i}|_{\{0\}\times X}\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
to
h_{i}|_{\{1\}\times X}\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
.
For each
i=0,1
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{i})=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{i}).

Also notice that for
i=0,1
we have

\displaystyle h_{0}|_{\{i\}\times X}=h_{1}|_{\{i\}\times X},

since
\partial I\times X\subset\overline{U}
.
Thus we get

\displaystyle\left[0,0,0,\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1})-\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0})\right]

\displaystyle=[S,h_{1}|_{\{0\}\times X},h_{1}|_{\{1\}\times X},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1})]-[S,h_{0}|_{\{0\}\times X},h_{0}|_{\{1\}\times X},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0})]

\displaystyle=0,

as desired.
This proves that the composition in (
4.22
) is zero.

To complete the proof of exactness of (
4.22
), take any element
\eta\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d)
such that
a(\eta)=[0,0,0,\eta]=0
in the group
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
.
This means that there exists an element

x\in\widehat{Z}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
such that
x+a(\eta)
is also an element in
\widehat{Z}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
.
This implies that, under the description
x=[S,h_{0},h_{1},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I})]
where
h_{I}
is a homotopy between
h_{0}
and
h_{1}
, there exists another homotopy
h^{\prime}_{I}
between
h_{0}
and
h_{1}
such that

(4.24)

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h^{\prime}_{I})=\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I})+\eta\mod(\mathrm{Im}(d)).

Notice that we have

\displaystyle h_{I}|_{\partial I\times X\cup I\times Y}=h^{\prime}_{I}|_{\partial I\times X\cup I\times Y}.

We may replace each of
h_{I}
and
h^{\prime}_{I}
by a homotopic one so that they satisfy

\displaystyle h_{I}|_{\overline{U}}=h^{\prime}_{I}|_{\overline{U}}.

By (
3.87
), the equality (
4.24
) is preserved by this replacement.
Now we have the element

(4.25)

\displaystyle[S,h^{\prime}_{I},h_{I},0]\in\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(I\times X,\overline{U}).

We have

(4.26)

\displaystyle R[S,h^{\prime}_{I},h_{I},0]=\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h^{\prime}_{I})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I}).
