By (
4.24
), (
4.26
) and the definition of
\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}
, we see that the element (
4.25
) maps to
\eta
under the left arrow in (
4.22
).
This completes the proof of the exactness of (
3
) at
\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d)
.

Finally we prove the exactness of (
3
) at
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
.
The equality
I\circ a=0
is obvious.
Take any element
[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\in\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
such that
I[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]=[S,h_{0},h_{1}]=0
in
{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
.
It is enough to consider the case
(S,h_{0},h_{1})\in Z_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
.
Then there exists a homotopy
h_{I}
between
h_{0}
and
h_{1}
which is constant on
Y
.
Moreover, we can choose
h_{I}
so that it is smooth.
Then we have

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]=[S,h_{0},h_{1},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I})]+a(\eta-\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I}))=a(\eta-\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I})).

This completes the proof of the exactness of (
3
) at
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
and finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Thus, together with Fact
2.24
we get the following.

Theorem 4.27
.

The quadruple
\left(\widehat{KO}^{A}_{+},R,I,a\right)
is a differential extension of the
KO
-theory
KO^{A}_{+}\simeq KO^{\mathrm{type}(A)}
on
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
.

Let
V:=V^{0}\oplus V^{1}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded real vector space with a positive definite inner product on each
V^{i}
.
We denote the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading operator on
V
by
\gamma_{V}
.
Since the algebra
\mathrm{End}(V)
is negligible,
Theorem
4.27
in particular means that
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}
and
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathrm{End}(V)\widehat{\otimes}A}
are both differential extensions of
KO^{\mathrm{type}(A)}
.
Actually, we have a canonical isomorphism
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}\simeq\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathrm{End}(V)\widehat{\otimes}A}
as follows.

Proposition 4.28
.

In the above settings, we have a natural isomorphism

(4.29)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}^{A}_{+}\simeq\widehat{KO}^{\mathrm{End}(V)\widehat{\otimes}A}_{+}

which is compatible with the corresponding isomorphisms of other functors appearing in Definition
4.6
via the structure homomorphisms (under the isomorphism
\mathrm{Ori}(A)\simeq\mathrm{Ori}(\mathrm{End}(V)\widehat{\otimes}A)
given by
u\mapsto\gamma_{V}\widehat{\otimes}u
).

Proof.

The natural isomorphism (
4.29
) on an object
(X,Y)
in
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
is given by the following refinement of the untwisted version of the map (
2.35
),

(4.30)

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\mapsto[E\otimes S,\psi^{E}(h_{0}),\psi^{E}(h_{1}),\eta].

The well-definedness and the compatibility with the structure maps follow from Lemma
3.91
.
By the exactness of (
3
) and the five lemma, we see that (
4.30
) defines an isomorphism, as desired.
∎

In particular we get

Corollary 4.31
.

The isomorphism class of the differential extension

\left(\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A},R,I,a\right)

of
KO^{\mathrm{type}(A)}
only depends on the type of
A
.

4.2.
The twisted groups
\widehat{KO}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}

In this subsection we give a model
\widehat{KO}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}
for the twisted differential
KO
-theory groups, by refining Definition
2.28
.
First we introduce the category
\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}
of twists on
\widehat{KO}_{+}
.

Definition 4.32

(
\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}
)
.

(1)

For each object
X\in\mathrm{Mfd}_{f}
, we define
\mathfrak{Twist}_{\widehat{KO}_{+},X}
to be the groupoid of smooth bundles of nondegenerate simple central graded
*
-algebras
\mathcal{A}
over
X
, where morphisms are ismorphisms between such bundles.

(2)

For each morphism
f\colon X\to X^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Mfd}_{f}
, we define a functor
f^{*}\colon\mathfrak{Twist}_{\widehat{KO}_{+},X^{\prime}}\to\mathfrak{Twist}_{\widehat{KO}_{+},X}
by the pullback.

(3)

We define the category
\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}
such that an object
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})
consists of
(X,Y)\in\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
and
\mathcal{A}\in\mathfrak{Twist}_{\widehat{KO}_{+},X}
and a morphism from
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})
to
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
consists of a morphism
f\colon(X,Y)\to(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
and an isomorphism
\mathcal{A}\simeq f^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.

We have the forgetful functor

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}\to\mathfrak{Twist}_{KO_{+}}^{2}.

Definition 4.33
.

Let
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})\in\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}
.

•

A
\widehat{KO}_{+}
-cocycle

(\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta)
on
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})
consists of an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\not{S}
with an inner product, a connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{A}
, a self-adjoint
\mathcal{A}
-connnection
\nabla^{\not{S}}
on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
, two smooth sections
h_{0},h_{1}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
such that
h_{0}|_{Y}=h_{1}|_{Y}
, and an element

\eta\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))/\mathrm{Im}(d)
.

•

Two
\widehat{KO}_{+}
-cocycle
(\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta)
and
(\not{S}^{\prime},(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})^{\prime},\nabla^{\not{S}^{\prime}},h^{\prime}_{0},h^{\prime}_{1},\eta^{\prime})
are
isomorphic

if we have
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}=(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})^{\prime}
and
\eta=\eta^{\prime}
, and there exists an isometric isomorphism of smooth
\mathcal{A}
-module bundles
f\colon\not{S}\simeq\not{S}^{\prime}
such that
f^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}^{\prime}}=\nabla^{\not{S}}
and
f\circ h_{i}=h^{\prime}_{i}\circ f
for
i=0,1
.

Definition 4.34

(
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
)
.

Let
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})\in\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}
.

•

We introduce an abelian monoid structure on the set
\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)
of isomorphism classes of
\widehat{KO}_{+}
-cocycles
(\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta)
by

\displaystyle[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta]+[\not{S}^{\prime},(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})^{\prime},\nabla^{\not{S}^{\prime}},h^{\prime}_{0},h^{\prime}_{1},\eta^{\prime}]

\displaystyle\quad=[\not{S}\oplus\not{S}^{\prime},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}\oplus(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})^{\prime},\nabla^{\not{S}}\oplus\nabla^{\not{S}^{\prime}},h_{0}\oplus h^{\prime}_{0},h_{1}\oplus h^{\prime}_{1},\eta+\eta^{\prime}].

•

We define
\widehat{Z}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)
to be the submonoid of
\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)
consisting of elements of the form

\displaystyle[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})],

where
h_{I}
is a smooth homotopy between
h_{0}
and
h_{1}
which is constant on
Y
, i.e., a smooth section
h_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
with
h_{I}|_{\{i\}\times X}=h_{i}
for
i=0,1
and
h_{I}|_{\{t\}\times Y}=h_{0}|_{Y}
for all
t\in I
(see (
3.86
)).

•

We define
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y):=\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)/\widehat{Z}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)
.

Lemma 4.35
.

\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
is an abelian group.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the untwisted case (Lemma
4.3
).
The additive inverse of an element
[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\in\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
is given by

(4.36)

-[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\\
=\left[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{1},h_{0},-\eta+\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}\oplus\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}\oplus\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\right].

Here
h_{I}
is a homotopy between
h_{0}\oplus h_{1}
and
h_{1}\oplus h_{0}
on
\not{S}\oplus\not{S}
.
∎

Thus we get the functor

(4.37)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}_{+}\colon\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}\to\mathrm{Ab}.

The structure homomorphisms in the twisted case are given as follows.

Definition 4.38

(Structure homomorphisms for
\widehat{KO}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}
)
.

We define the following structure homomorphisms, which are natural in
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})\in\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}
.

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\colon\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\to\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}_{\mathrm{clo}}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))

\displaystyle[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\mapsto\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})+d\eta.

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\colon\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\to KO_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)

\displaystyle[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\mapsto[\not{S},h_{0},h_{1}].

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\colon\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))/\mathrm{Im}(d)\to\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)

\displaystyle\eta\mapsto[0,0,0,0,0,\eta].

The well-definedness of
R
follows from (
3.84
) and (
3.85
).
The well-definedness of
a
and
I
is obvious.

Recall that, by (
3.45
),

\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
acts on the set of pairs
(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
.

Lemma 4.39
.

For any
KO^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}
-cycle
(\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta)
and any element
C\in\Omega^{1}(X;\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
, we have the following equality in
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
.

\displaystyle[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta]=[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+C,\nabla^{\not{S}}+C,h_{0},h_{1},\eta].

In particular, if we choose and fix a connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{A}
, then any element in
\widehat{KO}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}
is represented by a
\widehat{KO}_{+}
-cocycle of the form
(\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta)
.

Proof.

By (
4.36
), it is enough to prove that the following
\widehat{KO}_{+}
-cocycle is in
\widehat{Z}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)
.

\displaystyle[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+C,\nabla^{\not{S}}+C,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]

\displaystyle\quad+\left[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{1},h_{0},-\eta+\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}\oplus\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}\oplus\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\not{S}\oplus\not{S},(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+C)\oplus\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},(\nabla^{\not{S}}+C)\oplus\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0}\oplus h_{1},h_{1}\oplus h_{0},\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\left.\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}\oplus\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}\oplus\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\right].

Here
h_{I}
is any homotopy between
h_{0}\oplus h_{1}
and
h_{1}\oplus h_{0}
on
\not{S}\oplus\not{S}
.
Indeed, this claim follows from

\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}\oplus\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}\oplus\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\\
=\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I};(\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}+C)\oplus\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},(\nabla^{\not{S}}+C)\oplus\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)

as a result of Lemma
3.88
.
∎

Theorem 4.40
.

Let
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})\in\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}
.

(1)

We have
R\circ a=d
.

(2)

The following diagram commutes.

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 29.25833pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.47917pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 32.81439pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.2097pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.66168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{I}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern
