3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.25833pt\raise-33.32336pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.25833pt\raise-33.32336pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

(3)

The following sequence is exact.

\textstyle{KO_{+}^{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{top}}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))/\mathrm{Im}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I}
\textstyle{KO_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Proof.

(1) is obvious.
(2) follows from Theorem
3.96
.
The proof of (3) is analogous to the untwisted case in Theorem
4.7
(3), so we leave the details to the reader.
∎

We use the axiom of differential extensions of twisted generalized cohomology theories given in
[
BS12
]
.
By Theorem
4.40
, we get the following.

Theorem 4.45
.

The quadruple
\left(\widehat{KO}_{+},R,I,a\right)
is a differential extension of the twisted
KO
-theory
{KO}_{+}
on
\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}
.

Now we investigate into the dependence of
\widehat{KO}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}
on the bundle
\mathcal{A}
.
Recall that the twist of topological
KO
-theory is classified by
(\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A}),w(\mathcal{A}))\in\mathrm{GBrO}(X)=\mathbb{Z}_{8}\times HO(X)
(Corollary
2.36
).
Actually the same classification holds for our differential refinement.
We start with the following Proposition corresponding to Fact
2.33
.

Proposition 4.46
.

Let
(X,Y)
be an object of
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
.
Let
E=E^{0}\oplus E^{1}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded vector bundle over
X
with fiberwise positive definite inner product on each
E^{i}
.
For any smooth bundle of nondegenerate simple central graded
*
-algebras
\mathcal{A}
, we have a canonical isomorphism

(4.47)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)\simeq\widehat{KO}^{\mathrm{End}(E)\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)

which is compatible with the corresponding isomorphisms of other functors appearing in Definition
4.38
via the structure homomorphisms
(under the isomorphism
\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\mathrm{Ori}(\mathrm{End}(E)\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{A})
given by
u\mapsto\gamma_{E}\widehat{\otimes}u
).

Proof.

Fix an orthogonal connection
\nabla^{E}
on
E
, and denote by
\nabla^{\mathrm{End}(E)}
the induced connection on
\mathrm{End}(E)
.
The isomorphism (
4.47
) is given by the following refinement of the map (
2.35
),

(4.48)

[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\\
\mapsto[E\otimes\not{S},\nabla^{\mathrm{End}(E)}\otimes 1+1\otimes\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{E}\otimes 1+1\otimes\nabla^{\not{S}},\psi^{E}(h_{0}),\psi^{E}(h_{1}),\eta].

The well-definedness and the compatibility with the structure maps follow from Lemma
3.91
.
By the exactness of (
3
) and the five lemma, we see that (
4.48
) defines an isomorphism.
Actually, the right hand side of (
4.48
) is independent of the choice of
\nabla_{E}
by Lemma
4.39
.
Thus we see that (
4.39
) defines a canonical isomorphism (
4.47
) independent of any additional choice, as desired.
∎

Thus we get the following differential version of Corollary
2.36
.

Corollary 4.49
.

Given an object
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})\in\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}
, the isomorphism class of the twisted differential
KO
-theory group
\widehat{KO}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)
only depends on the class of
\mathcal{A}
in
\mathrm{GBrO}(X)
.

5.
The model in terms of skew-adjoint operators

In this section, we introduce another model of (twisted) differential
KO
-theory,
\widehat{KO}_{-}^{A}
(
\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}
), in terms of skew-adjoint operators.
In this section we work over
\mathbb{R}
, unless otherwise stated (e.g., in Remarks
5.7
and
5.19
).

5.1.
Algebraic preparations

This subsection is devoted to algebraic preparations.

Definition 5.1

(
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A
)
.

Let
A
be a simple central graded
*
-algebra.
We define
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A
to be
\Sigma^{0,1}A=A\widehat{\otimes}Cl_{0,1}
as a
*
-algebra, and introduce a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
grading on
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A
by
1\otimes\gamma
, where
\gamma
is the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading operator on
Cl_{0,1}
.

Thus we have

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A)^{0}

\displaystyle=\{a\widehat{\otimes}1\ |\ a\in A\},

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A)^{1}

\displaystyle=\{a\widehat{\otimes}\beta\ |\ a\in A\}.

Example 5.2
.

In the case where
A=Cl_{p,q}
for nonnegative integers
p
and
q
, we have
\Sigma^{0,1}A=Cl_{p,q+1}
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A\simeq Cl_{q,p+1}
.
An isomorphism of
*
-algebras
\Sigma^{0,1}A\simeq\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A
does not preserve the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading:

\displaystyle Cl_{p,q+1}

\displaystyle\simeq Cl_{q,p+1}

\displaystyle\beta_{0}

\displaystyle\mapsto\beta^{\prime}_{0}

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}

\displaystyle\mapsto\beta^{\prime}_{0}\beta^{\prime}_{i}\ (1\leq i\leq p)

\displaystyle\beta_{j}

\displaystyle\mapsto\beta^{\prime}_{0}\alpha^{\prime}_{j}\ (1\leq j\leq q)

where we denoted by
\alpha_{*}
(
\alpha^{\prime}_{*}
) and
\beta_{*}
(
\beta^{\prime}_{*}
) the negative and positive Clifford generators of
Cl_{p,q+1}
(
Cl_{q,p+1}
), respectively.

Lemma 5.3
.

For any simple central graded
*
-algebra
A
, the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded
*
-algebra
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A
is a simple central graded
*
-algebra with

\displaystyle\mathrm{type}(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A)=-\mathrm{type}(A)-1.

If
u\in A
is a volume element of
A
, the element
u\otimes\beta^{\mathrm{type}(A)+1}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A
is a volume element of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A
.

Proof.

The proof can be given by a case-by-case check of Table
1
, and we leave the details to the reader.
One possible simplification is to use the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading-preserving isomorphism
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\Sigma^{0,1}A\simeq\Sigma^{1,0}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A
to reduce the problem to the case
\mathrm{type}(A)=0
.
∎

The following lemma can also be proved easily.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
A
be a simple central graded
*
-algebra
and
S
be a
\Sigma^{0,1}A
-module with an inner product.
We also regard
S
as a
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A
-module.
Then we have a bijection

(5.5)

\displaystyle\psi_{\beta}\colon\mathrm{End}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}(S)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{End}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A}(S)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto\beta^{|f|_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}}f

which preserves the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
grading.
Here
|f|_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
in the right hand side of (
5.5
) is the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading of
f
as an element in
\mathrm{End}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}(S)
.
Moreover, the bijection restricts to the following bijections relating
\mathrm{Self}
with
\mathrm{Skew}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Self}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}(S)\simeq\mathrm{Skew}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A}(S),\quad

\displaystyle\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}(S)\simeq\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A}(S),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Skew}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}(S)\simeq\mathrm{Self}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A}(S),\quad

\displaystyle\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}(S)\simeq\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A}(S).

Applying Lemma
5.4
fiberwise, we also get the bijection in the bundle case,

(5.6)

\displaystyle\psi_{\beta}\colon\mathrm{End}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{End}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}).

Remark 5.7
.

In the
\mathbb{C}
-linear setting, we simply have the bijection

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{End}^{1}_{A}(S)\simeq\mathrm{End}^{1}_{A}(S),\ f\mapsto\sqrt{-1}f,

which transforms
\mathrm{Skew}
to
\mathrm{Self}
.
We use this bijection when we relate
K_{+}
with
K_{-}
in Section
6
.

5.2.
The topological model
KO_{-}

In this subsection we define another model of the (twisted)
KO
-theory in terms of skew-adjoint operators.
The functor
KO_{-}^{A}
is shown to be a model of
KO^{-\mathrm{type}(A)-2}
.

We start with the untwisted groups.
The definition of
KO_{-}
is given by simply replacing
\mathrm{Self}
with
\mathrm{Skew}
in Definitions
2.20
and
2.21
of untwisted
KO_{+}
, as follows.

Definition 5.9
.

Let
(X,Y)
be a finite CW-pair and
A
be a simple central graded
*
-algebra.

•

A
triple

(S,m_{0},m_{1})
on
(X,Y)
consists of an
A
-module
S
with an inner product and two continuous maps
m_{0},m_{1}\in\mathrm{Map}(X,\mathrm{Skew}_{A}^{*}(S))
with
m_{0}|_{Y}=m_{1}|_{Y}
.
Such
m_{i}
’s are called
mass terms
on
S
(c.f. Subsubsection
1.1.1
).

•

Triples
(S,m_{0},m_{1})
and
(S^{\prime},m^{\prime}_{0},m^{\prime}_{1})
on
(X,Y)
are
isomorphic
if there exists an isometric isomorphism of
A
-modules
f\colon S\simeq S^{\prime}
such that
f\circ m_{i}=m^{\prime}_{i}\circ f
for
i=0,1
.

Definition 5.10

(
KO_{-}^{A}(X,Y)
)
.

Let
(X,Y)
and
A
be as above.

•

On the set
M^{p,q}_{-}(X,Y)
of isomorphism classes of triples
(S,m_{0},m_{1})
on
(X,Y)
, we introduce an abelian monoid structure by the direct sum.

•

We define
Z^{A}_{-}(X,Y)
to be the submonoid of
M^{A}_{-}(X,Y)
consisting of isomorphism classes of triples
(S,m_{0},m_{1})
on
(X,Y)
such that there exists a homotopy between
m_{0}
and
m_{1}
which is constant on
Y
, i.e., a map
m_{I}\in\mathrm{Map}(I\times X,\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{A}(S))
with
m_{I}|_{\{i\}\times X}=m_{i}
for
i=0,1
and
m_{I}|_{\{t\}\times Y}=m_{0}|_{Y}
for all
t\in I
.

•

We define
KO^{A}_{-}(X,Y)=M^{A}_{-}(X,Y)/Z^{A}_{-}(X,Y)
to be the quotient monoid.

Next we define twisted groups.
The category of twists
\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{{KO}_{-}}
in
{KO}_{-}
is just the same as that for
{KO}_{+}
in Definition
2.26
,

(5.11)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{{KO}_{-}}:=\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{{KO}_{+}}.

The definition of
{KO}_{-}
is given by modifiying Definition
2.28
for twisted
KO_{+}
in the same way, so we go briefly.

Definition 5.12

(
KO^{\mathcal{A}}_{-}(X,Y)
)
.

Let
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})\in\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{{KO}_{-}}
.
By replacing
\mathrm{Self}
to
\mathrm{Skew}
in Definition
2.28
, we define the abelian group
KO^{\mathcal{A}}_{-}(X,Y)
.

Thus elements in
KO^{\mathcal{A}}_{-}(X,Y)
is of the form
[\not{S},m_{0},m_{1}]
with
m_{0},m_{1}\in\Gamma(X,\mathrm{Skew}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(S))
such that
m_{0}|_{Y}=m_{1}|_{Y}
.
We have
[\not{S},m_{0},m_{1}]=0
if
m_{0}
and
m_{1}
are homotopic relative to
Y
.

The result corresponding to Fact
2.33
also holds for
KO_{-}
.
For
E=E^{0}\oplus E^{1}
and any
\mathcal{A}
as in the setting there, we have a canonical isomorphism using Lemma
2.12
,

(5.13)

\displaystyle KO^{\mathcal{A}}_{-}(X,Y)\simeq KO^{\mathrm{End}(E)\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{A}}_{-}(X,Y).

We can relate
KO_{-}
with
KO_{+}
using the results in Subsection
5.1
.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})\in\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{{KO}_{-}}
.
We have an isomorphism

(5.15)

\displaystyle KO_{-}^{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\simeq KO_{+}^{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(X,Y),

which is natural in
(X,Y)
and
\mathcal{A}
.

Proof.

The map (
5.15
) is given by

\displaystyle[\not{S},m_{0},m_{1}]\mapsto[\not{S},\psi_{\beta}(m_{0}),\psi_{\beta}(m_{1})],

where
\psi_{\beta}\colon\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})\simeq\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})
is the isomorphism in (
5.6
).
The well-definedness and the fact that this map gives a natural isomorphism are obvious.
∎

Since we have
\Sigma^{0,1}\Sigma^{1,0}\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}\hat{\otimes}Cl_{1,1}
and
Cl_{1,1}
is negligible, by Proposition
5.14
and (
5.13
), we have a natural isomorphism

(5.16)

\displaystyle KO_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\simeq KO_{+}^{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\Sigma^{1,0}\mathcal{A}}(X,Y).

Thus we see that the two models
KO_{+}
and
KO_{-}
are equivalent under explicit algebraic operations.
In particular, in the case of untwisted groups, also using Lemma
5.3
we have the following result corresponding to Fact
2.24
.

Proposition 5.17
.

Let
A
be a simple central graded
*
-algebra.
We have a natural isomorphism on the category of finite CW-pairs,

(5.18)

\displaystyle KO_{-}^{A}\simeq KO^{-\mathrm{type}(A)-2}.

Remark 5.19
.

In the
\mathbb{C}
-linear setting, just replacing the coefficients
\mathbb{R}
by
\mathbb{C}
in Definitions
5.10
and
5.12

as we did in Remark
2.37
,
we define groups
K_{-}^{A}(X,Y)
and
K_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
.
In this case, we have an isomorphism

(5.20)

\displaystyle K_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}\simeq K_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}

by
[\not{S},m_{0},m_{1}]\mapsto[\not{S},\sqrt{-1}m_{0},\sqrt{-1}m_{1}]
(see Remark
5.7
).
By (
2.38
) and (
5.20
), we have a natural isomorphism on the category of finite CW-pairs,

(5.21)

\displaystyle K_{-}^{A}\simeq K^{\mathrm{type}(A)}.

5.3.
The Pontryagin character forms for mass terms

Recall that the definition of the differential model
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{A}
used the Pontryagin character forms
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
for invertible elements
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
.
In this subsection, we define their skew-adjoint variants.

5.3.1.
The case of trivial bundles

Here we explain the skew-adjoint version of Subsubsection
3.2.2
.
Let
X
be a manifold and
A
be nondegenerate.
We define the
Pontryagin character form

\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m)
for an invertible element (a mass term)
m\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{A}(S))
.

For such
m
, consider the section
tm\in C^{\infty}((0,\infty)\times X,\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{A}(S))
, where
t
is the coordinate in
(0,\infty)
.
We have

(5.22)

\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(d_{(0,\infty)\times X}+tm;d)=dt\wedge\mathrm{Tr}_{A}\left(me^{td_{X}m+t^{2}m^{2}}\right)\\
\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Here the first term is defined in (
3.67
), in which
d
is the trivial connection on
\underline{A}
.
Since
m^{2}
is strictly negative, the convergence result analogous to Lemma
3.13
holds and allows us to define the following.

Definition 5.23

(
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m)
)
.

For
m\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{A}(S))
, its
Pontryagin character form

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m)\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(A)-2}_{\mathrm{clo}}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(A))

is defined by

(5.24)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m)

\displaystyle:=-\pi^{-1/2}\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(d_{(0,\infty)\times X}+tm)

\displaystyle=-\pi^{-1/2}\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}dt\wedge\mathrm{Tr}_{A}\left(me^{td_{X}m+t^{2}m^{2}}\right).

We also define

Definition 5.25

(
\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{I})
)
.

For
m_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{A}(S))
, we define its
Chern-Simons form
by

(5.26)

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{I}):=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}\left(m_{I}\right)\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(A)-3}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(A)).

5.3.2.
The general case

Now we explain the skew-adjoint version of Subsection
3.4
.
Let
X
,
\mathcal{A}
,
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\not{S}
with an inner product be as in that subsection, where we always assume that the fibers of
\mathcal{A}
are nondegenerate.
Let
\nabla^{\not{S}}
be a skew-adjoint
\mathcal{A}
-
connection
on
\not{S}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
In this setting, given a smooth invertible section
m\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Skew}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we are going to define its
Pontryagin character form

\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
.

On the manifold
(0,\infty)\times X
,
We consider the skew-adjoint
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+tm
on
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\not{S}
compatible with
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+tm;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}((0,\infty)\times X;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Using the convergence result analogous to Lemma
3.77
, we define the following.

Definition 5.27

(
\mathrm{Ph}(m;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
,
\mathrm{CS}(m_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})
)
.

Let
X
,
\mathcal{A}
,
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\not{S}
be as above.

(1)

For any smooth invertible section
m\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Skew}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we define

(5.28)

\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}):=-\pi^{-1/2}\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+tm;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})\\
\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-2}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))

(2)

For any smooth invertible section
m_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{Skew}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we define

(5.29)

\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}):=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}\left(m_{I};\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\\
\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-3}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

5.3.3.
The relation between
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}
and
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}

In Subsection
5.1
, we saw that we have an isomorphism
\psi_{\beta}\colon\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})\simeq\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})
.
In this subsection, we show that the Pontryagin character forms
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}
and
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}
are also related under this isomorphism.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
be a bundle of simple central graded
*
-algebras equipped with a connection.
The induced connection on
\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}
are denoted by
\Sigma^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
, respectively.
Assume that a
\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\not{S}
is equipped with an inner product and let
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
be a
\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}
-superconnection compatible with
\Sigma^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
Then decompose
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
as in (
3.40
),

\displaystyle\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}=\nabla+\sum_{j}\omega_{j}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j},

where
\nabla
is an
\mathcal{A}
-connection,
\omega_{j}\in\Omega^{j}(X)
and
\xi_{j}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
with
|\omega_{j}|+|\xi_{j}|=1\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.
Then consider the following operator on
\Omega^{*}(X;\not{S})

(5.30)

\displaystyle\psi_{\beta}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}):=\nabla+\sum_{j}(-1)^{\nu(j)}\omega_{j}\widehat{\otimes}\psi_{\beta}(\xi_{j}),

where

(5.31)

\displaystyle\nu(j):=\begin{cases}0&j\equiv 0,1\pmod{4}\\
1&j\equiv 2,3\pmod{4}.\end{cases}

The expression (
5.30
) does not depend on the decomposition (
3.40
).

Lemma 5.32
.

The operator
\psi_{\beta}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})
in (
5.30
) is a
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}
-superconnection on
\not{S}
compatible with
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
Moreover, if
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
is a skew-adjoint superconnection, then
\psi_{\beta}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})
is a self-adjoint superconnection.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the fact that
\psi_{\beta}
preserves the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading.
The second statement follows from a degree-wise check by using the definition of self/skew-adjointness of superconnections (Definition
3.60
).
∎

Proposition 5.33
.

In the above setting, assume that
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
is skew-adjoint.
For any element
f\in\mathbb{R}[[z]]
, we have

(5.34)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(f(F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\Sigma^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})))=\mathrm{Tr}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(f(-F(\psi_{\beta}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}});\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})))

\displaystyle\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})),

under the following identifications of orientation bundles,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Ori}(\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}),

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto u\widehat{\otimes}\beta,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Ori}(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}),

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{\nu(\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A}))}u\widehat{\otimes}\beta^{\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})+1},

where
\nu
is given by (
5.31
).

Proof.

The both sides of (
5.34
) are closed forms of degree
-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})\pmod{4}
by Proposition
3.63
and Lemma
5.3
.

In this proof, we denote

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle:=F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\Sigma^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}

\displaystyle:=F(\psi_{\beta}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}});\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}).

Also, for elements
\Xi\in\Omega^{*}(X;\mathrm{End}(\not{S}))
, we denote by
\Xi=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{4}}\Xi_{j}
the decomposition with respect to the form-degree modulo
4
, in which
\Xi_{j}\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}+j}(U;\mathrm{End}(\not{S}))
.

It is enough to prove in the case that
f(z)=z^{n}
for each
n
.
We may work locally, and use the local expression as in (
3.43
).
As explained there, we may write
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}=d+C
for some
C\in\Omega^{1}(U;A^{0}_{\mathrm{skew}})
, and
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}=d+C+B
for some
B\in\Omega^{*}(U;\mathrm{End}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))^{1}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\psi_{\beta}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})=d+C+\beta\cdot B_{0}+B_{1}-\beta\cdot B_{2}-B_{3}.

Here we denote
\beta\cdot(\omega\widehat{\otimes}\xi):=\omega\widehat{\otimes}(\beta\xi)
for
\omega\widehat{\otimes}\xi\in\Omega^{*}(U;\mathrm{End}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
.
Let us denote
\widetilde{B}:=\beta\cdot B_{0}+B_{1}-\beta\cdot B_{2}-B_{3}
.
Then we have (
3.48
)

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle=dB+B^{2},

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}

\displaystyle=d\widetilde{B}+\widetilde{B}^{2}.

Using
\beta\cdot B_{j}\cdot=(-1)^{|j|+1}B_{j}\cdot\beta\cdot
as operators on
\Omega^{*}(U;\mathrm{End}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
, we can easily check that

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{0}

\displaystyle=-F_{0},

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{1}

\displaystyle=\beta\cdot F_{1},

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{2}

\displaystyle=F_{2},

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\beta\cdot F_{3}.

It holds that
\beta\cdot F_{j}\cdot=(-1)^{|j|}F_{j}\cdot\beta\cdot
since
F\in\Omega^{*}(U;\mathrm{End}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))^{0}
.
Using this formula, we can check that

\displaystyle((-\widetilde{F})^{n})_{0}

\displaystyle=(F^{n})_{0},

\displaystyle((-\widetilde{F})^{n})_{1}

\displaystyle=-\beta\cdot(F^{n})_{1},

\displaystyle((-\widetilde{F})^{n})_{2}

\displaystyle=-(F^{n})_{2},

\displaystyle((-\widetilde{F})^{n})_{3}

\displaystyle=\beta\cdot(F^{n})_{3},

by an induction on
n
.
As we have checked at the beginning of the proof, the both sides of (
5.34
) are forms of degree
-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})\pmod{4}
. Using this fact, we see that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{u\widehat{\otimes}\beta}(F^{n})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}_{u\widehat{\otimes}\beta}((F^{n})_{-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}_{(-1)^{\nu(\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A}))}u\widehat{\otimes}\beta^{\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})+1}}(((-\widetilde{F})^{n})_{-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}_{(-1)^{\nu(\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A}))}u\widehat{\otimes}\beta^{\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})+1}}((-\widetilde{F})^{n}),

as desired.
∎

By Proposition
5.33
, we get the following.

Corollary 5.35
.

Let
X
and
(\mathcal{A},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
be as above and
\not{S}
be a
\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}
-module bundle with inner product.
Then for any skew-adjoint
\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
compatible with
\Sigma^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
, we have

(5.36)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\Sigma^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})=\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\psi_{\beta}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}});\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}).

On the manifold
I\times X
, for any skew-adjoint
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}(\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A})
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}
compatible with
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}(\Sigma^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
, we have

(5.37)

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I};\Sigma^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})=\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(\psi_{\beta}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I});\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}).

Here the orientation bundles are identified as in Proposition
5.33
.

Using this corollary, we also get the correspondence of the Pontryagin character forms for mass terms and gradations.

Corollary 5.38
.

Let
X
and
(\mathcal{A},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
be as above and
\not{S}
be a
\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}
-module bundle with inner product, equipped with a skew-adjoint
\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}
-connection
\nabla^{\not{S}}
.
Then for any element
m\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
, we have

(5.39)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m;\Sigma^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})=\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\psi_{\beta}(m);\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}),

in which the identity
\psi_{\beta}(\nabla^{\not{S}})=\nabla^{\not{S}}
is used. For any element
m_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{Skew}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we also have

(5.40)

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{I};\Sigma^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})=\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(\psi_{\beta}(m_{I});\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}).

Here the orientation bundles are identified as in Proposition
5.33
.

5.4.
The differential model

5.4.1.
The untwisted groups
\widehat{KO}_{-}^{A}(X,Y)

Now we define the untwisted differential
KO
-theory
\widehat{KO}_{-}
.
As in the case for topological
KO_{-}
, the definition is given by just replacing
\mathrm{Self}
to
\mathrm{Skew}
and changing the form degree from
(4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1)
to
(4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(A)-3)
in Definitions
4.1
and
4.2
of
\widehat{KO}_{+}
, as follows.

Definition 5.41
.

Let
A
be a nondegenerate simple central graded
*
-algebra.
Let
(X,Y)
be an object in
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
.

•

A
\widehat{KO}_{-}
-cocycle

(S,m_{0},m_{1},\eta)
on
(X,Y)
consists of an
A
-module
S
with an inner product, two smooth maps
m_{0},m_{1}\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Skew}_{A}^{*}(S))
such that
m_{0}|_{Y}=m_{1}|_{Y}
, and an element

\eta\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(A)-3}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d).

•

Two
\widehat{KO}_{-}
-cocycles
(S,m_{0},m_{1},\eta)
and
(S^{\prime},m^{\prime}_{0},m^{\prime}_{1},\eta^{\prime})
are
isomorphic

if there exists an isometric isomorphism of
A
-modules
f\colon S\simeq S^{\prime}
such that
f\circ m_{i}=m^{\prime}_{i}\circ f
for
i=0,1
, and we have
\eta=\eta^{\prime}
.

Definition 5.42

(
\widehat{KO}_{-}^{A}(X,Y)
)
.

Let
A
and
(X,Y)
be as in Definition
5.41

•

We introduce an abelian monoid structure on the set
\widehat{M}^{A}_{-}(X,Y)
of isomorphism classes of
\widehat{KO}_{-}
-cocycles
(S,m_{0},m_{1},\eta)
on
(X,Y)
by

\displaystyle[S,m_{0},m_{1},\eta]+[S^{\prime},m^{\prime}_{0},m^{\prime}_{1},\eta^{\prime}]=[S\oplus S^{\prime},m_{0}\oplus m^{\prime}_{0},m_{1}\oplus m^{\prime}_{1},\eta+\eta^{\prime}].

•

We define
\widehat{Z}^{A}_{-}(X,Y)
to be the submonoid of
\widehat{M}^{A}_{-}(X,Y)
consisting of elements of the form

\displaystyle[S,m_{0},m_{1},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{I})],

where
m_{I}
is a smooth homotopy between
m_{0}
and
m_{1}
which is constant on
Y
, i.e., a smooth map
m_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{A}(S))
with
m_{I}|_{\{i\}\times X}=m_{i}
for
i=0,1
and
m_{I}|_{\{t\}\times Y}=m_{0}|_{Y}
for all
t\in I
.

•

We define
\widehat{KO}^{A}_{-}(X,Y):=\widehat{M}^{A}_{-}(X,Y)/\widehat{Z}^{A}_{-}(X,Y)
.

In a way similar to the proof of Lemma
4.3
, we can show that
\widehat{KO}^{A}_{-}(X,Y)
is an abelian group, and thus
\widehat{KO}^{\mathcal{A}}
defines a functor
\mathrm{MfdPair}^{\mathrm{op}}_{f}\to\mathrm{Ab}
.

Definition 5.43

(Structure homomorphisms for
\widehat{KO}^{A}_{-}
)
.

We define the following structure homomorphisms, which are natural in
(X,Y)
.

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\colon\widehat{KO}_{-}^{A}(X,Y)\to\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(A)-2}_{\mathrm{clo}}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))

\displaystyle[S,m_{0},m_{1},\eta]\mapsto\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{1})-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{0})+d\eta.

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\colon\widehat{KO}_{-}^{A}(X,Y)\to KO_{-}^{A}(X,Y)

\displaystyle[S,m_{0},m_{1},\eta]\mapsto[S,m_{0},m_{1}].

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\colon\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(A)-3}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d)\to\widehat{KO}_{-}^{A}(X,Y)

\displaystyle\eta\mapsto[0,0,0,\eta].

We have the natural isomorphism corresponding to Proposition
4.28
,

(5.44)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}^{A}_{-}\simeq\widehat{KO}^{\mathrm{End}(V)\widehat{\otimes}A}_{-},

by sending
[S,m_{0},m_{1},\eta]
to
[E\otimes S,\psi^{E}(m_{0}),\psi^{E}(m_{1}),\eta]
.

Moreover, we have the following refinement of the untwisted version of Proposition
5.14
.

Proposition 5.45
.

We have a natural isomorphism

(5.46)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\Sigma^{0,1}A}\simeq\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A}

which is compatible with the corresponding isomorphisms of other functors appearing in Definitions
5.43
and
4.6
via the structure homomorphisms.
Here we use Proposition
5.14
and the identification
\mathrm{Ori}(\Sigma^{0,1}A)\simeq\mathrm{Ori}(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}A)
in Proposition
5.33
.

Proof.

The transformation (
5.46
) on an object
(X,Y)
in
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
is given by

\displaystyle[S,m_{0},m_{1},\eta]\mapsto[S,\psi_{\beta}(m_{0}),\psi_{\beta}(m_{1}),\eta],

where
\psi_{\beta}\colon\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}(S)\simeq\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}{A}}(S)
is the isomorphism in (
5.6
).
The well-definedness follows from Corollary
5.38
.
Since
\psi_{\beta}
is an isomorphism, we easily see that this map gives a natural isomorphism.
∎

Since we have
\Sigma^{0,1}\Sigma^{1,0}A=A\hat{\otimes}Cl_{1,1}
and
Cl_{1,1}
is negligible, by Proposition
5.14
and (
5.13
), we have a natural isomorphism

(5.47)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}_{-}^{A}\simeq\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\Sigma^{1,0}A},

which is compatible with the structure homomorphisms.
Recall that by Theorem
4.27
we know that
(\widehat{KO}^{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\Sigma^{1,0}A}_{+},R,I,a)
is a differential extension of
KO^{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\Sigma^{1,0}A}_{+}\simeq KO^{-\mathrm{type}(A)-2}
.
Thus we get the following.

Theorem 5.48
.

The quadruple
\left(\widehat{KO}^{A}_{-},R,I,a\right)
is a differential extension of the
KO
-theory
KO^{A}_{-}\simeq KO^{-\mathrm{type}(A)-2}
on
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
.

The natural isomorphism (
5.47
), being an explicit algebraic manipulation, allows us to translate results on
\widehat{KO}_{+}
into corresponding ones on
\widehat{KO}_{-}
.

5.4.2.
The twisted groups
\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)

Now we define the twisted groups
\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
.
Again the category
\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{-}}
of twists for
\widehat{KO}_{-}
is just the same as that for
\widehat{KO}_{+}
,

(5.49)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{-}}:=\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{+}}.

The definition of the twisted
\widehat{KO}_{-}
is also a simple modification of Definition
4.34
, so we go briefly.

Definition 5.50

(
\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
)
.

Let
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})\in\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{-}}
.
Replacing
\mathrm{Self}
by
\mathrm{Skew}
and changing the form degree from
(4\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1)
to
(4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(A)-3)
in Definition
4.34
, we define the abelian group
\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
.

An element of
\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
is of the form
[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},m_{0},m_{1},\eta]
, where
m_{i}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Skew}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
are such that
m_{0}|_{Y}=m_{1}|_{Y}
, and

\eta\in\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-3}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))/\mathrm{Im}(d).

We have
[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},m_{0},m_{1},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})]=0
for a homotopy
m_{I}
from
m_{0}
to
m_{1}
relative to
Y
.
We clearly have the functor

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}_{-}\colon\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{-}}\to\mathrm{Ab}.

Definition 5.51

(Structure homomorphisms for
\widehat{KO}^{\mathcal{A}}_{-}
)
.

We define the structure homomorphisms

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\colon\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\to\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-2}_{\mathrm{clo}}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})),

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\colon\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\to KO_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y),

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\colon\Omega^{4\mathbb{Z}-\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-3}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))/\mathrm{Im}(d)\to\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)

applying the same modification as in Definition
5.50
to Definition
4.38
.

As a counterprt of Proposition
4.46
, we have an isomorphism

(5.52)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}^{\mathcal{A}}_{-}(X,Y)\simeq\widehat{KO}^{\mathrm{End}(E)\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{A}}_{-}(X,Y),

which are compatible with the structure homomorphisms.
Moreover, as in the untwisted case (Proposition
5.45
), we get the refinement of Proposition
5.14
as follows.

Proposition 5.53
.

We have an isomorphism

(5.54)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\simeq\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)

which is compatible with the corresponding isomorphisms of other functors appearing in Definitions
5.51
and
4.38
via the structure homomorphisms.
Here we use Proposition
5.14
and the identification
\mathrm{Ori}(\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A})\simeq\mathrm{Ori}(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A})
in Proposition
5.33
.

Proof.

Take a connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{A}
.
Recall that, by Lemma
4.39
, any element in
\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
is represented by a
\widehat{KO}_{+}
-cocycle of the form
(\not{S},\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},h_{0},h_{1},\eta)
.
The
\widehat{KO}_{-}
-version of the lemma is shown by exactly the same proof, so we can represent any element in
\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
by a
\widehat{KO}_{-}
-cocycle of the form
(\not{S},{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},m_{0},m_{1},\eta)
.

Then, the homomorphism (
5.54
) is given by

\displaystyle[\not{S},{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},m_{0},m_{1},\eta]\mapsto[\not{S},\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},\psi_{\beta}(m_{0}),\psi_{\beta}(m_{1}),\eta],

where
\psi_{\beta}\colon\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{\Sigma^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})\simeq\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\mathcal{A}}(\not{S})
is the isomorphism in (
5.6
).
The well-definedness follows from Corollary
5.38
, and the resulting map is independent on the choice of
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
by Lemma
4.39
and its
\widehat{KO}_{-}
-version.
Since
\psi_{\beta}
is an isomorphism, we easily see that this map gives an isomorphism.
∎

The above results give a twisted generalization of (
5.47
)

(5.55)

\displaystyle\widehat{KO}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)\simeq\widehat{KO}_{+}^{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{0,1}\Sigma^{1,0}\mathcal{A}}(X,Y),

which is compatible with the structure homomorphisms.
Recall that by Theorem
4.45
we know that
(\widehat{KO}_{+},R,I,a)
is a differential extension of the twisted
KO
-theory
KO_{+}
.
Thus we get the following.

Theorem 5.56
.

The quadruple
\left(\widehat{KO}_{-},R,I,a\right)
is a differential extension of the twisted
KO
-theory
KO_{-}
on
\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{KO}_{-}}
.

Again, the natural isomorphism (
5.55
) allows us to translate results on twisted
\widehat{KO}_{+}
into corresponding ones on twisted
\widehat{KO}_{-}
.

6.
The complex case :
\widehat{K}_{+}
and
\widehat{K}_{-}

So far we have worked in the
\mathbb{R}
-linear setting.
In this section, we explain that the
\mathbb{C}
-linear version of the above story works by straightforward modifications, producing differential extensions of the Karoubi’s
K
-theory
K_{+}
(Remark
2.37
).

6.1.
The complex superconnections

In this subsection we explain the
\mathbb{C}
-linear version of Section
3
.
In the
\mathbb{C}
-linear setting, we work with simple central graded
*
-algebras over
\mathbb{C}
.
The traces
\mathrm{Tr}_{u}
and
\mathrm{Tr}_{A}
are defined exactly as in Subsection
3.1
, by replacing
\dim_{\mathbb{R}}
with
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}
.

We explain the
complex superconnection formalism
by modifying Subsection
3.3
.
We start with a bundle of nondegenerate simple central graded
*
-algebras
\mathcal{A}
over
\mathbb{C}
and an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\not{S}
with an inner product on a manifold.
Then we define
\mathcal{A}
-superconnections
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
, the curvature
F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})
, the self/skew-adjointness of superconnections exactly in the same way as in that subsection.
We easily see that the obvious
\mathbb{C}
-linear version of the results until Remark
3.61
hold.
In the
\mathbb{C}
-linear case, we have (recall
\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})\in H^{0}(X;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
in the complex settings)

(6.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(f(F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})))\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})+1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}).

for a
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
, and any
f\in\mathbb{C}[[z]]
.
For
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
and
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I}
self/skew-adjoint, we define

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(e^{\mp F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})})\in\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})+1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle:=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}/\mathrm{skew}}\left(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S},I};\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}\right)\in\Omega^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}).

Now we move on to the constructions corresponding to Subsections
3.4
and
5.3
.
Recall the endomorphism
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{C}}
on
\mathbb{C}
-valued differential forms defined in (
2.58
).
Given
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and a self-adjoint (=skew-adjoint)
\mathcal{A}
-connection
\nabla^{\not{S}}
, for an invertible section
h\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
,
we consider the manifold
(0,\infty)_{t}\times X
with the self-adjoint superconnection
\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th
, and
define its
Chern character form
by

(6.2)

\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}):=-\pi^{-1/2}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{C}}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+th;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})\\
\in\Omega^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Note that
\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})
is a
real
line bundle.
Thus it can be nontrivial, but can be checked directly, that the formula (
6.2
) defines an element in
\Omega^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A}))
.

Similarly for
m\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Skew}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we define

(6.3)

\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}):=-\pi^{-1/2}(-\sqrt{-1})^{\deg}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{C}}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{skew}}(\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}+tm;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}})\\
\in\Omega^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Here
(-\sqrt{-1})^{\deg}
is an operator on
\mathbb{C}
-valued differential forms which multiplies
d
-forms by
(-\sqrt{-1})^{d}
.
For a homotopy
h_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we define the
Chern-Simons form
by

(6.4)

\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}):=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}\left(h_{I};\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\\
\in\Omega^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

Similarly for
m_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{Skew}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we define

(6.5)

\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}):=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{skew}}\left(m_{I};\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\nabla^{\not{S}}\right)\\
\in\Omega^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})-1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

As a special case of the above constructions applied to trivial bundles
\underline{A}
and
\underline{S}
, we get the
\mathbb{C}
-linear version of Subsection
3.2
and Subsubsection
5.3.1
.
We use the corresponding notations such as
\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
for
h\in\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{A}(S)
.

We can show that the analogous properties of Chern character forms
\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
corresponding to those listed in Subsection
3.2.3
.
The invariance under tensoring negligible modules (Lemma
3.23
) can be shown in the same way.
The compatibility with the suspension (Proposition
3.27
) is also basically the same, but we need to take care of the multiplications by
(\sqrt{-1})^{\bullet}
appearing in the definition (
2.58
) of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{C}}
.
The statement becomes the following.

Proposition 6.6
.

For any element
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}_{\Sigma A}^{\dagger}(S))
, we define
\widetilde{h}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{\dagger}(S))
by the same formula as (
3.25
).
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)=\int_{I}\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(\widetilde{h})

under the identification of orientation bundles (cf. (
2.2
))

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ori}(A)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Ori}(\Sigma A),

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}u\widehat{\otimes}\beta,&(\mathrm{type}(A)=0)\\
\sqrt{-1}u\widehat{\otimes}\beta.&(\mathrm{type}(A)=1)\end{array}\right.

To show that the Chern character forms represent the correct cohomology classes, we need the result corresponding to Theorem
3.29
about the Chern character forms for the universal (tautological) gradation
h_{\mathrm{univ}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S),\mathrm{Self}^{*}_{A}(S))
.
The topological Chern character homomorphism
(
2.53
) can be regarded as an element

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{top}}\in{H}^{2\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(A)}\left(K_{\mathrm{type}(A)},\{*\};\mathbb{R}\right).

We claim that the equality

(6.7)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rham}\left(\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})\right)=\iota_{S,u}^{*}\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{top}}

holds in

H^{2\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(A)}\left(\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S),\{\beta\};\mathbb{R}\right)
.
Indeed, the proof of Theorem
3.29
can be modified to the
K
-theory.
In that proof, we used the fact that the degree-zero part of the topological Pontryagin character is realized as the Chern-Weil construction on the real Grassmannians, and used the diffeomorphism
\mathrm{Self}^{\dagger}_{A}(S_{n})\simeq\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
in (
2.49
) to compare
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})
with
\mathcal{R}\circ\mathrm{Tr}(e^{-\nabla_{\mathrm{Gr}}^{2}})
.
The complex analogue of this argument works since the topological Chern character is realized as the Chern-Weil construction on the complex Grassmannians
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{C}^{2n})
, and we have a diffeomorphism among models of the
K
-theory spectrum corresponding to that detailed in the study of the
KO
-spectrum in Subsubsection
2.3.2
.
These results imply the following one, corresponding to Corollary
3.31
.

Corollary 6.8
.

Let
A
be a simple central graded
*
-algebra over
\mathbb{C}
and
(X,Y)
be an object of
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
.
Represent classes of
K_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
by triples
(S,h_{0},h_{1})
with
h_{i}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S))
as in Remark
2.37
. Then the topological Chern character homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{top}}\colon K_{+}^{A}(X,Y)\to H^{2\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(A)}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))

is given by

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1}]\mapsto\mathrm{Rham}\left(\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1})-\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0})\right).

Using this result on untwisted Chern character forms, we also get the corresponding statement about the twisted case.
Recall that, for a CW-pair
(X,Y)
, we have the twisted Chern character homomorphism,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{top}}\colon K^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}(X,Y)\to H^{2\mathbb{Z}+{\mathrm{type}}(\mathcal{A})}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(\mathcal{A})).

We then have the following result corresponding to Corollary
3.100
.

Corollary 6.9
.

let
(X,Y)
be an object of
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
, and
\mathcal{A}
and
\not{S}
over
\mathbb{C}
as above.
Suppose we have two smooth sections
h_{0},h_{1}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Self}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
such that
h_{0}|_{Y}=h_{1}|_{Y}
.
Then we have

(6.10)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{top}}([\not{S},h_{0},h_{1}])=\mathrm{Rham}\left(\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})-\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})\right).

Now we turn to the discussion corresponding to Subsubsection
5.3.3
, relating
\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}
and
\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{skew}}
.
In the
\mathbb{C}
-linear setting here, the argument is simpler.
Recall that we have a bijection
\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{A}(S)\simeq\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S)
by
m\mapsto\sqrt{-1}m
(Remarks
5.7
and
5.19
). Let us decompose a skew-adjoint
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
as (
3.43
),

\displaystyle\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}=\nabla+\sum_{j}\omega_{j}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j},

where
\omega_{j}\in\Omega^{j}(X)
and
\xi_{j}\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
with
|\omega_{j}|+|\xi_{j}|=1\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.
Then consider the following operator on
\Omega^{*}(X;\not{S})

(6.11)

\displaystyle\psi_{\sqrt{-1}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}):=\nabla+\sum_{j}(-\sqrt{-1})^{j-1}\omega_{j}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j}.

We easily see that the expression (
6.11
) is independent of the decomposition and defines a self-adjoint
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
.
We have the following result corresponding to Proposition
5.33
, whose proof is an easy computation checking the case
f(z)=z^{n}
and so omitted.

Proposition 6.12
.

For a skew-adjoint
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
compatible with
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}
and for any
f\in\mathbb{C}[[z]]
, we have the equality

(6.13)

\displaystyle f(F(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}))=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\deg}\circ f(-F(\psi_{\sqrt{-1}}(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}});\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}))

in
\Omega^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(\mathcal{A})+1}(X;\mathrm{Ori}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})
.
Here
(-\sqrt{-1})^{\deg}
is the operator on differential forms which multiplies
d
-forms by
(-\sqrt{-1})^{d}
.

Thus we conclude:

Corollary 6.14
.

For any element
m\in C^{\infty}(X;\mathrm{Skew}^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}(\not{S}))
, we have

(6.15)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})=\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(\sqrt{-1}m;\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}).

For any element
m_{I}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X;\mathrm{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{Skew}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\not{S}))
, we have

(6.16)

\displaystyle\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{I};\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}})=\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(\sqrt{-1}(m_{I});\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}}).

6.2.
The definitions of differential
K
-theories
\widehat{K}_{+}
and
\widehat{K}_{-}

Now it should be obvious to the reader that the definitions of the differential extensions of
K_{+}
and
K_{-}
are given by just replacing the coefficient from
\mathbb{R}
to
\mathbb{C}
in the definitions of
\widehat{KO}_{+}
and
\widehat{KO}_{-}
, and by using the
\mathbb{C}
-linear version of the characteristic forms defined in the last subsection.

Definition 6.17

(
\widehat{K}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
and
\widehat{K}_{-}^{A}(X,Y)
)
.

Let
A
be a nondegenerate simple central graded
*
-algebra over
\mathbb{C}
and let
(X,Y)
be an object in
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
.
Replacing the coefficient
\mathbb{R}
by
\mathbb{C}
and the form degree
4\mathbb{Z}
by
2\mathbb{Z}
and using characteristic forms in Subsection
6.1
, we define
\widehat{K}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
and
\widehat{K}_{-}^{A}(X,Y)
, respectively.

Thus, elements of the group
\widehat{K}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)
are of the form
[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]
, where
h_{i}\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}_{A}^{*}(S))
are such that
h_{0}|_{Y}=h_{1}|_{Y}
and

\eta\in\Omega^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d).

We have
[S,h_{0},h_{1},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{I})]=0
when
h_{I}
is a homotopy relative to
Y
between
h_{0}
and
h_{1}
.
Similarly, elements of the group
\widehat{K}_{-}^{A}(X,Y)
are of the form
[S,m_{0},m_{1},\eta]
, where
m_{i}\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Skew}_{A}^{*}(S))
are such that
m_{0}|_{Y}=m_{1}|_{Y}
and

\eta\in\Omega^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d).

We have
[S,m_{0},m_{1},\mathrm{CS}_{\mathrm{skew}}(m_{I})]=0
when
m_{I}
is a homotopy relative to
Y
between
m_{0}
and
m_{1}
.

For twisted groups, we define the (same) categories
\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{K}_{+}}=\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{K}_{-}}
by the
\mathbb{C}
-linear analogue of Definition
4.32
.
We define the following.

Definition 6.18

(
\widehat{K}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
and
\widehat{K}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
)
.

Let
(X,Y,\mathcal{A})\in\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{K}_{+}}=\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{K}_{-}}
.
By the same replacement as in Definition
6.17
of Definitions
4.34
and
5.50
, we define
\widehat{K}_{+}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
and
\widehat{K}_{-}^{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
, respectively.

Definition 6.19

(Structure homomorphisms for
\widehat{K}_{+}
and
\widehat{K}_{-}
)
.

We define the structure homomorphisms
R
,
I
,
a
for
\widehat{K}^{A}_{+}
,
\widehat{K}^{\mathcal{A}}_{+}
,
\widehat{K}^{A}_{-}
and
\widehat{K}^{\mathcal{A}}_{-}
by the same replacement as in Definition
6.17
of Definitions
4.6
,
4.38
,
5.43
and
5.51
, respectively.

For example in the case
\widehat{K}^{A}_{+}
we define

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\colon\widehat{K}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)\to\Omega_{\mathrm{clo}}^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\mapsto\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{1})-\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{0})+d\eta.

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\colon\widehat{K}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)\to K_{+}^{A}(X,Y)

\displaystyle[S,h_{0},h_{1},\eta]\mapsto[S,h_{0},h_{1}].

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\colon\Omega^{2\mathbb{Z}+\mathrm{type}(A)-1}(X,Y;\mathrm{Ori}(A))/\mathrm{Im}(d)\to\widehat{K}_{+}^{A}(X,Y)

\displaystyle\eta\mapsto[0,0,0,\eta].

We have natural isomorphisms for both untwisted and twisted cases corresponding to Propositions
5.45
and
5.53

(6.20)

\displaystyle\widehat{K}_{-}\simeq\widehat{K}_{+},

which are compatible with the structure homomorphisms.
The isomorphisms (
6.20
) are given by
[S,m_{0},m_{1},\eta]\mapsto[S,\sqrt{-1}m_{0},\sqrt{-1}m_{1},\eta]
in the untwisted case and
[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},m_{0},m_{1},\eta]\mapsto[\not{S},\nabla^{\mathcal{A}},\nabla^{\not{S}},\sqrt{-1}m_{0},\sqrt{-1}m_{1},\eta]
in the twisted case.
The well-definedness follows from Corollary
6.14
.

Moreover, by the cohomological properties of the Chern character forms
\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{self}}
in Corollaries
6.8
and
6.9
, we can check the axioms of differential cohomology theories as in Theorems
4.7
and
4.40
.
Thus we conclude the following.

Theorem 6.21
.

The quadruples
\left(\widehat{K}^{A}_{+},R,I,a\right)
and
\left(\widehat{K}^{A}_{-},R,I,a\right)
are differential extensions of the
K
-theories
K^{A}_{+}\simeq K^{A}_{-}\simeq KO^{\mathrm{type}(A)}
on
\mathrm{MfdPair}_{f}
.

The quadruples
\left(\widehat{K}_{+},R,I,a\right)
and
\left(\widehat{K}_{-},R,I,a\right)
are differential extensions of the twisted
K
-theories
{K}_{+}\simeq K_{-}
on
\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{K}_{+}}=\mathfrak{Twist}^{2}_{\widehat{K}_{-}}
.

7.
The proofs

7.1.
The proof of Proposition
3.27

Here we prove Proposition
3.27
.
Let
S
be a
\Sigma^{0,1}A
-module. For
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}^{\dagger}(S))
, we have the corresponding
\widetilde{h}\in C^{\infty}(I\times X,\mathrm{Self}^{\dagger}_{A}(S))
.
Choose a volume element
u
of
A
to trivialize
\mathrm{Ori}(A)
.

By Definition
3.15
, using
h^{2}=1
and
\widetilde{h}^{2}=1
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)

\displaystyle=\pi^{-1/2}\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}dte^{-t^{2}}\mathrm{Tr}_{u\widehat{\otimes}\beta}(h\exp(-tdh)),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\widetilde{h})

\displaystyle=\pi^{-1/2}\mathcal{R}\circ\int_{(0,\infty)}dte^{-t^{2}}\mathrm{Tr}_{u}(\widetilde{h}\exp(-td\widetilde{h})).

We compute (note that we are working in the algebra
\Omega^{*}(I\times X,\mathrm{End}_{A}(S))
with the multiplication (
3.33
)),

\displaystyle\exp(-td\widetilde{h})

\displaystyle=\exp(-t(d\theta\cdot(\partial_{\theta}\widetilde{h})+(\sin\pi\theta)dh))

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-t)^{n}}{n!}(d\theta\cdot(\partial_{\theta}\widetilde{h})+(\sin\pi\theta)dh)^{n}

\displaystyle=d\theta\cdot(\partial_{\theta}\widetilde{h})\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-t)^{n+1}}{n!}\cdot(\sin^{n}\pi\theta)(dh)^{n}\right)+\exp(-t(\sin\pi\theta)dh).

Here the last equality used the fact that
dh
commutes with
d\theta\cdot(\partial_{\theta}\widetilde{h})
, which can be checked easily by
h\in C^{\infty}(X,\mathrm{Self}_{\Sigma^{0,1}A}^{\dagger}(S))
and the formula for
\widetilde{h}
.
We also note

\displaystyle\widetilde{h}(\partial_{\theta}\widetilde{h})=\pi(\beta\cos\pi\theta+h\sin\pi\theta)(-\beta\sin\pi\theta+h\cos\pi\theta)=\pi\beta h.

Thus we have (note that
\widetilde{h}d\theta=-d\theta\widetilde{h}
)

(7.1)

\int_{I}\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\widetilde{h})\\
=\pi^{1/2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{n!}\int_{0}^{\infty}(-t)^{n+1}e^{-t^{2}}dt\int_{I}\sin^{n}(\pi\theta)\cdot\mathcal{R}\left(d\theta\mathrm{Tr}_{u}(-\beta{h}(d{h})^{n})\right)\right).

Now assume
\mathrm{type}(A)
is even.
In this case
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\widetilde{h})
is an even form and
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
is an odd form, so that (
7.1
) becomes

(7.2)

\displaystyle\pi^{1/2}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{(2l+1)!}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2l+2}e^{-t^{2}}dt\int_{I}\sin^{2l+1}(\pi\theta)\cdot\mathcal{R}\left(d\theta\mathrm{Tr}_{u}(-\beta h(dh)^{2l+1})\right)\right),

and
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
is expanded as

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)=-\pi^{-1/2}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(2l+1)!}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2l+1}e^{-t^{2}}dt\cdot\mathcal{R}\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{u\widehat{\otimes}\beta}(h(dh)^{2l+1})\right).

We now check that (
7.2
) and (
7.3
) coincide.
We have:

(7.4)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{u}(-\beta h(dh)^{2l+1})=-\mathrm{Tr}_{u\widehat{\otimes}\beta}(h(dh)^{2l+1}),

(7.5)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2l+2}e^{-t^{2}}dt\int_{I}d\theta\sin^{2l+1}(\pi\theta)=\frac{(2l+1)!!\sqrt{\pi}}{2^{l+2}}\cdot\frac{(2l)!!\cdot 2}{(2l+1)!!\pi}=\frac{l!}{2\sqrt{\pi}},

(7.6)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2l+1}e^{-t^{2}}dt=\frac{l!}{2}.

Using the above formulas and (
2.57
), we see that (
7.2
) and (
7.3
) coincide.
This finishes the proof in the case where
\mathrm{type}(A)
is even.

Now assume
\mathrm{type}(A)
is odd.
In this case
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(\widetilde{h})
is an odd form and
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
is an even form, so that (
7.1
) becomes

(7.7)

\displaystyle-\pi^{1/2}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{(2l)!}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2l+1}e^{-t^{2}}dt\int_{I}\sin^{2l}(\pi\theta)\mathcal{R}\left(d\theta\mathrm{Tr}_{u}(-\beta h(dh)^{2l})\right)\right),

and
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)
is expanded as

(7.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h)=\pi^{-1/2}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(2l)!}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2l}e^{-t^{2}}dt\cdot\mathcal{R}\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{u\widehat{\otimes}\beta}(h(dh)^{2l})\right).

We now check that (
7.7
) and (
7.8
) coincide.
In this case we have

(7.9)

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Tr}_{u}(-\beta h(dh)^{2l+1})=2\mathrm{Tr}_{u\widehat{\otimes}\beta}(h(dh)^{2l+1}),

(7.10)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2l+1}e^{-t^{2}}dt\int_{I}\sin^{2l}(\pi\theta)d\theta=\frac{l!}{2}\cdot\frac{(2l-1)!!}{(2l)!!}=\frac{(2l-1)!!}{2^{l+1}},

(7.11)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2l}e^{-t^{2}}dt=\frac{(2l-1)!!\sqrt{\pi}}{2^{l+1}}.

Using above formulas and (
2.57
), we see that (
7.7
) and (
7.8
) coincide.
This finishes the proof in the case where
\mathrm{type}(A)
is odd and completes the proof of Proposition
3.27
.

7.2.
Proof of Theorem
3.29

Here we prove Theorem
3.29
.
The first step is to show the following.

Lemma 7.12
.

Theorem
3.29
holds if
\mathrm{type}(A)=0
.

Proof.

Choose and fix a volume element
u\in A
to trivialize
\mathrm{Ori}(A)
.
Recall that, in Subsubsection
2.3.2
, we explained the model of the
KO
-spectrum realized as a direct limit of
\mathrm{Skew}^{\dagger}_{A}(-)
, and in the case that
\mathrm{type}(A)=0
we gave an explicit homeomorphism (
2.52
) with the model of
KO_{0}
realized by using Grassmannians.
Also, as explained in Subsection
2.4
, the degree-zero part of the topological Pontryagin character is realized as the Chern-Weil construction on vector bundles.
For each
n
, set
S_{n}:=(S_{+}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{n})\oplus(S_{-}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{n})
in the notation there. Recall the diffeomorphism (
2.49
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Self}^{\dagger}_{A}(S_{n})=\mathrm{Self}^{\dagger}_{A}((S_{+}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{n})\oplus(S_{-}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{n}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})

\displaystyle u\otimes a

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Im}((1-a)/2)=\mathrm{Im}(P),

where
P:=(1-a)/2
. The canonical connection on the tautological real vector bundle
\theta_{2n}
over
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
is given by

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathrm{Gr}}=PdP,

where the inclusions
\Omega^{*}(\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{R}^{2n});\theta_{2n})\subset\Omega^{*}(\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{R}^{2n});\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
are understood.
We know that the topological Pontryagin character is the direct limit with respect to
n
of the Pontryagin character form
\mathcal{R}\circ\mathrm{Tr}(e^{\nabla_{\mathrm{Gr}}^{2}})-n
for the connection
\nabla_{\mathrm{Gr}}
.
Thus it is enough to show that (see (
2.47
))

(7.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\circ\mathrm{Tr}(e^{\nabla_{\mathrm{Gr}}^{2}})-n-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})\in\mathrm{Im}(d)

on
\mathrm{Self}^{\dagger}_{A}(S_{n})
for each
n
.

First we compute
\mathrm{Tr}(e^{\nabla_{\mathrm{Gr}}^{2}})
.
We have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathrm{Gr}}^{2}=PdP\wedge dP=\frac{1}{4}Pda\wedge da.

Using the relation
ada+da\cdot a=0
and
P^{2}=P
,
we have

(7.14)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}(e^{\nabla_{\mathrm{Gr}}^{2}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2k+1}k!}\left(\mathrm{Tr}(-a(da)^{2k})+\mathrm{Tr}((da)^{2k})\right).

Next we compute
\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})
.
Applying Definition
3.15
to
h_{\mathrm{univ}}=u\otimes a
and using
h_{\mathrm{univ}}^{2}=1
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})=\mathcal{R}\circ\pi^{-1/2}\int_{(0,\infty)}e^{-t^{2}}dt\wedge\mathrm{Tr}_{u}\left((u\otimes a)\cdot e^{-tu\widehat{\otimes}da}\right).

We have

\displaystyle e^{-tu\widehat{\otimes}da}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{2k}}{(2k)!}\cdot 1\widehat{\otimes}(da)^{2k}-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!}\cdot u\widehat{\otimes}(da)^{2k+1},

so that, using
\mathrm{Tr}_{u}(u\otimes B)=-\mathrm{Tr}(B)
and
\mathrm{Tr}_{u}(1\otimes B)=0
for any
B\in\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{u}\left((u\otimes a)e^{-tu\widehat{\otimes}da}\right)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{2k}}{(2k)!}\cdot\mathrm{Tr}\left(-a(da)^{2k}\right).

Using the formula

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t^{2}}t^{2k}dt=\sqrt{\pi}\cdot\frac{(2k-1)!!}{2^{k+1}},

we get

(7.15)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})=\mathcal{R}\circ\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2k+1}k!}\mathrm{Tr}\left(-a(da)^{2k}\right).

Now we can compute the element (
7.13
).
By (
7.14
) and (
7.15
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\circ\mathrm{Tr}(e^{\nabla_{\mathrm{Gr}}^{2}})-n-\mathrm{Ph}_{\mathrm{self}}(h_{\mathrm{univ}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}\circ\left(-n+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2k+1}k!}\mathrm{Tr}((da)^{2k})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}\circ\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}(e^{\frac{1}{4}da\wedge da}-1).

This is exact, so we have proved (
7.13
).
This completes the proof of Lemma
7.12
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.29
.

By Proposition
3.27
, we know that
\mathrm{Ph}(m)
is compatible with the suspension.
Thus we can reduce to the case
\mathrm{type}(A)=0
, and the result follows from Lemma
7.12
.
∎

7.3.
The proof of Proposition
3.63

Here we prove Proposition
3.63
.
We only give the proof for (1), whose straightforward modification leads to (2).

It is enough to consider the case that
f(z)=z^{n}
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
Moreover, working locally, we may assume that
V=\underline{A}
for some
A
and
\not{S}=\underline{S}
for an
A
-module
S
with an inner product.
Furthermore, by Lemma
3.59
it is enough to consider the case that
\nabla^{\mathcal{A}}=d
, since the action (
3.45
) preserves the self-adjointness.
Fix a volume element
u
for
A
to trivialize
\mathrm{Ori}(A)
.
Then we can decompose the self-adjoint
\mathcal{A}
-superconnection
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}
as

\displaystyle\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}=d+\sum_{j\in J}\omega_{j}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j},

for
\omega_{j}\in\Omega^{*}(X)
and
\xi_{j}\in\mathrm{End}_{A}(S)
with
|\omega_{j}|+|\xi_{j}|\equiv 1\pmod{2}
.
We have

\displaystyle F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}};d)=(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})^{2}=\sum_{j}d\omega_{j}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j}+\sum_{j,k}(\omega_{j}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j})\cdot(\omega_{k}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{k}).

Thus
(F({\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}}};d))^{n}=(\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla^{\not{S}})^{2n}
is the sum of the all possible terms of the form

(7.16)

\displaystyle(\alpha_{j_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{1}})\cdot(\alpha_{j_{2}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{2}})\cdot\cdots\cdot(\alpha_{j_{l}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l}}),

where
j_{t}\in J
and
\alpha_{j_{t}}
is either of
d\omega_{j_{t}}
or
\omega_{j_{t}}
for each
t
, and if the number of
t
such that
\alpha_{j_{t}}=d\omega_{j_{t}}
is
m
, we have
l+m=2n
.

We show that, for each term (
7.16
) as above, the sum

(7.17)

\mathrm{Tr}_{u}\left((\alpha_{j_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{1}})\cdot(\alpha_{j_{2}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{2}})\cdot\cdots\cdot(\alpha_{j_{l}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l}})\right)\\
+\mathrm{Tr}_{u}\left((\alpha_{j_{l}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l}})\cdot(\alpha_{j_{l-1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l-1}})\cdot\cdots\cdot(\alpha_{j_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{1}})\right)

can be nonzero only if we have
\sum_{t}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|\equiv\mathrm{type}(A)+1\pmod{4}
.
This implies the desired result.

Fix a term (
7.16
).
We define
I\subset\{1,\cdots,l\}
so that
\alpha_{j_{t}}=d\omega_{j_{t}}
for
t\in I
and
\alpha_{j_{t}}=\omega_{j_{t}}
for
t\notin I
.
We have
|I|=m
.
We also define
\sigma\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
by

\displaystyle\sigma(x):=\begin{cases}1&\mbox{if }x\equiv 1,2\pmod{4},\\
0&\mbox{if }x\equiv 0,3\pmod{4}.\end{cases}

Then the self-adjointness of
\nabla\!\!\!\!\nabla
(Definition
3.60
) implies that

(7.18)

\displaystyle\xi_{j}^{*}=(-1)^{\sigma(|\omega_{j}|)}\xi_{j}

for all
j\in J
.
Also, a straightforward computation shows that

(7.19)

\displaystyle u^{*}=(-1)^{\sigma(\mathrm{type}(A))}u.

Now we compute (
7.16
).
We define
A,B,C,D\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
by the following.

(7.20)

\displaystyle(\alpha_{j_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{1}})\cdot(\alpha_{j_{2}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{2}})\cdot\cdots\cdot(\alpha_{j_{l}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{A}\alpha_{j_{1}}\wedge\alpha_{j_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\alpha_{j_{l}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{1}}\xi_{j_{2}}\cdots\xi_{j_{l}},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{j_{l}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l}})\cdot(\alpha_{j_{l-1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l-1}})\cdot\cdots\cdot(\alpha_{j_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{1}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{B}\alpha_{j_{l}}\wedge\alpha_{j_{l-1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\alpha_{j_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l}}\xi_{j_{l-1}}\cdots\xi_{j_{1}},

\displaystyle\alpha_{j_{1}}\wedge\alpha_{j_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\alpha_{j_{l}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{C}\alpha_{j_{l}}\wedge\alpha_{j_{l-1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\alpha_{j_{1}},

\displaystyle(u\xi_{j_{1}}\xi_{j_{2}}\cdots\xi_{j_{l}})^{*}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{D}\xi_{j_{l}}\xi_{j_{l-1}}\cdots\xi_{j_{1}}u.

Then we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left(u\xi_{j_{1}}\xi_{j_{2}}\cdots\xi_{j_{l}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\left((u\xi_{j_{1}}\xi_{j_{2}}\cdots\xi_{j_{l}})^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{D}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\xi_{j_{l}}\xi_{j_{l-1}}\cdots\xi_{j_{1}}u\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{D}\mathrm{Tr}\left(u\xi_{j_{l}}\xi_{j_{l-1}}\cdots\xi_{j_{1}}\right),

so that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{u}\left(\xi_{j_{1}}\xi_{j_{2}}\cdots\xi_{j_{l}}\right)=(-1)^{D}\mathrm{Tr}_{u}\left(\xi_{j_{l}}\xi_{j_{l-1}}\cdots\xi_{j_{1}}\right).

Using this, we get

(7.21)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{u}\left((\alpha_{j_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{1}})\cdot(\alpha_{j_{2}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{2}})\cdot\cdots\cdot(\alpha_{j_{l}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l}})\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{A}\mathrm{Tr}_{u}\left(\xi_{j_{1}}\xi_{j_{2}}\cdots\xi_{j_{l}}\right)\alpha_{j_{1}}\wedge\alpha_{j_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\alpha_{j_{l}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{A+C+D}\mathrm{Tr}_{u}\left(\xi_{j_{l}}\xi_{j_{l-1}}\cdots\xi_{j_{1}}\right)\alpha_{j_{l}}\wedge\alpha_{j_{l-1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\alpha_{j_{1}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{A+B+C+D}\mathrm{Tr}_{u}\left((\alpha_{j_{l}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l}})\cdot(\alpha_{j_{l-1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{l-1}})\cdot\cdots\cdot(\alpha_{j_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}\xi_{j_{1}})\right).

Now we compute
A+B+C+D
.
We have

(7.22)

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\sum_{s<t}|\alpha_{j_{s}}|\cdot|\xi_{j_{t}}|,

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\sum_{t<s}|\alpha_{j_{s}}|\cdot|\xi_{j_{t}}|,

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=\sum_{s<t}|\alpha_{j_{s}}|\cdot|\alpha_{j_{t}}|,

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=\sum_{t}\sigma(|\omega_{j_{t}}|)+\sigma(\mathrm{type}(A)),

where the last equality used (
7.18
) and (
7.19
).
Recall that
|\xi_{j_{t}}|\equiv|\alpha_{j_{t}}|\pmod{2}
if
t\in I
and
|\xi_{j_{t}}|\equiv|\alpha_{j_{t}}|-1\pmod{2}
if
t\notin I
.
We get

(7.23)

\displaystyle A+B

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\neq s}|\alpha_{j_{s}}|\cdot|\xi_{j_{t}}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\neq s}|\alpha_{j_{s}}|\cdot|\alpha_{j_{t}}|-(l-m)\sum_{s\in I}|\alpha_{j_{s}}|-(l-m-1)\sum_{s\in J\setminus I}|\alpha_{j_{s}}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in J\setminus I}|\alpha_{j_{s}}|,

where the last equality follows from the fact that the first term in the second line is even, and that
l-m
is even since
l+m=2n
.
Using
\sigma(x-1)=\sigma(x)+x
and the fact that
|\omega_{j_{t}}|=|\alpha_{j_{t}}|-1
if
t\in I
and
|\omega_{j_{t}}|=|\alpha_{j_{t}}|
if
t\notin I
, we have

(7.24)

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\in J}\sigma(|\alpha_{j_{t}}|)+\sum_{t\in I}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|+\sigma(\mathrm{type}(A)).

Moreover, using
\sigma(x)+\sigma(y)+xy=\sigma(x+y)
, we get

(7.25)

\displaystyle C+D

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\sum_{t\in J}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|\right)+\sum_{t\in I}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|+\sigma(\mathrm{type}(A)).

By (
7.23
) and (
7.25
), we conclude

(7.26)

\displaystyle A+B+C+D=\sigma\left(\sum_{t\in J}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|\right)+\sum_{t\in J}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|+\sigma(\mathrm{type}(A)).

Recall that we already know that the map (
3.51
) preserves the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading if
\mathrm{type}(A)
is odd, and reverses the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading if
\mathrm{type}(A)
is even.
In particular, by Lemma
3.46
, we know that (
7.17
) can be nonzero only when

(7.27)

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in J}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|\equiv\mathrm{type}(A)+1\pmod{2}.

In this case (
7.26
) becomes

\displaystyle A+B+C+D

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\sum_{t\in J}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|\right)+\mathrm{type}(A)+1+\sigma(\mathrm{type}(A))

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\sum_{t\in J}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|\right)+\sigma(\mathrm{type}(A)+1)

where the last equality used
\sigma(x+1)=\sigma(x)+x+1
.
Using (
7.21
), the sum (
7.17
) can be nonzero only when both (
7.27
) and

\displaystyle\sigma\left(\sum_{t}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|\right)=\sigma(\mathrm{type}(A)+1)

are satisfied.
But
\sigma(x)=\sigma(y)
and
x\equiv y\pmod{2}
imply
x\equiv y\pmod{4}
, so these conditions imply

\displaystyle\sum_{t}|\alpha_{j_{t}}|\equiv\mathrm{type}(A)+1\pmod{4}.

Thus we get the desired result.
